EMAAR PROPERTIES PJSC
Emaar is recognised as one of the world’s most esteemed and valuable real estate 
developers. With a net asset value of approximately AED 247 billion1 and proven 
strengths across residential, retail, hospitality, and leisure, Emaar continues to 
shape modern lifestyles through intelligent design, operational excellence, and a 
steadfast focus on delivering an exceptional customer experience. Anchored by 
a forward-looking vision and a commitment to continuous innovation, we build 
integrated, future-ready environments that enhance quality of life and set new 
standards for the cities of tomorrow.

=== página 7
A Year of Strong Market 
Momentum
With non-oil growth driven by tourism, 
diversification and fiscal resilience, the 
UAE remains on a trajectory of balanced, 
long-term development. This stability 
provides a strong foundation for 
Emaar to expand its integrated lifestyle 
communities not only within Dubai, 
but also beyond it to select key markets, 
including Egypt and India, leveraging our 
proven master-planned model to diversify 
growth and capture opportunities 
in high-demand regions.
In 2025, Emaar delivered another 
year of strong performance across 
both domestic and international 
operations. We reported property sales 
of AED 80.4 billion, alongside continued 
expansion across our development 
pipeline and recurring-revenue assets. 
This performance reflects not only rising 
profitability, but also the growing scale, 
geographic breadth and resilience of 
our portfolio across residential, retail, 
hospitality and commercial segments. 
At its core, it underscores the sustained 
desirability of our communities, the 
enduring trust placed in the Emaar 
brand, and the ability of our development 
philosophy to travel successfully across 
markets and cycles.
Keeping Community at the Heart 
of Development
Community remains central to everything 
we do. In a year nationally recognised 
as the ‘Year of Community’, our purpose 
gained greater resonance. For Emaar, 
community is where development begins. 
Across our destinations, we create spaces 
that encourage interaction, shared 
identity, and a sense of belonging.
This commitment is powerfully reflected 
each year at Downtown Dubai, where the 
New Year’s Eve celebration at the Burj 
Khalifa unfolds before a global audience 
of millions. Broadcast worldwide and 
watched across continents, it stands 
among the most viewed celebrations 
anywhere in the world—a moment that 
captures Dubai’s ambition, creativity, and 
openness to diverse communities who 
come to inhabit and celebrate its spirit.
A Strategy Defined by Disciplined 
Focus and Execution
Our strategy remains anchored in 
disciplined execution, long-term value 
creation, and purposeful expansion. 
Supported by a strong balance sheet, 
we continue to invest selectively—
strengthening our land bank, expanding 
recurring-revenue assets, and enhancing 
BUILDING THE  
PLACES OF THE FUTURE
Dear Shareholders,
The year 2025 was a defining year for 
Emaar—one that reinforced who we are, 
what we stand for, and the responsibility 
we carry as creators of some of the world’s 
most recognisable urban destinations. 
At Emaar, we believe the places we create 
shape how people live, connect and 
belong. Our work is not simply about 
elevating everyday life; it is about leading 
culture, shaping taste and creating new 
aspirations for generations to come.
As aspirations evolve globally, people are 
seeking new ways of living—environments 
that support well-being, flexibility and a 
deeper sense of community. We continue 
to challenge long-held assumptions about 
space, experience and purpose, asking 
fundamental questions about how cities 
should serve the people who inhabit them.
This conviction has guided Emaar 
from the very beginning. Long before 
integrated, smart community living 
became a global aspiration, we pioneered 
a development philosophy rooted in 
connection, quality and long-term 
value. In 2025, we continued to build on 
this foundation. The year marked both 
continuity and momentum—extending 
what we have shaped over decades while 
sharpening our focus on what comes 
next. Demand for walkable, mixed-use 
destinations that integrate living, working 
and leisure continues to deepen, driven by 
both domestic and international buyers. 
Our master-planned communities remain 
firmly aligned with this shift, guided by 
principles that have consistently defined 
our leadership and will continue to shape 
our future growth.
We advanced our Climate Risk Framework 
and Decarbonisation Roadmap to embed 
climate considerations into operations and 
align our pathway with the UAE Net Zero 
2050 vision.

=== página 8
our branded residential and hospitality 
offerings. A growing backlog provides 
clear visibility into future revenues and 
reinforces confidence in the resilience and 
sustainability of our growth.
In 2025, we accelerated the integration 
of ESG across the Group, strengthening 
policies and systems to translate our 
ambition into measurable action that 
drives long-term success. We advanced 
our Climate Risk Framework and 
Decarbonisation Roadmap to embed 
climate considerations into operations 
and align our pathway with the UAE 
Net Zero 2050 vision. The year saw us 
undertake several initiatives to reduce and 
divert construction and operational waste 
and strengthen recycling and circular 
practices across our assets.
Our people, communities, and governance 
remain central to our long-term 
success. We continued investing in 
employee development, health, safety, 
and well-being. During the year, we 
strengthened Emaar’s governance 
framework by updating the Corporate 
Governance Policy and key ethics-
related policies, including Anti-Fraud 
and Whistleblower frameworks and the 
introduction of a new Human Rights 
framework, reinforcing regulatory 
oversight, internal controls, and alignment 
with international best practices. Aligned 
with the UAE’s ‘Year of Community’, we 
also supported initiatives that promote 
education, inclusion, and well-being 
across the communities we serve.
As we shape future urban landscapes, 
we remain committed to sustainable 
growth, measured not only by financial 
performance, but also by the positive 
impact we create for people, communities, 
and the environment.
A Future Shaped by Promise and 
Purpose
At Emaar, we continue to build places for 
the future—destinations that anticipate the 
lifestyles of tomorrow. Developments such 
as The Heights Country Club & Wellness, 
Emaar Estate, Rashid Marina & Yacht 
District, and new phases of The Oasis and 
the Grand Polo Club & Resort exemplify this 
ambition. This momentum is reinforced 
by a growing land bank that supports 
future development and earnings visibility, 
alongside an established international 
presence across select markets worldwide, 
extending the reach of our integrated 
community model beyond the UAE. 
Together, these foundations position Emaar 
to deliver sustained profitability through 
disciplined execution, scale, and enduring 
demand for the quality of places we create.
On behalf of the Board, I extend my sincere 
gratitude to our customers, partners, 
employees, and shareholders. Your 
continued trust and confidence strengthen 
our resolve. Together, we will continue 
building environments that elevate 
lives across the world and contribute 
meaningfully to the UAE’s remarkable 
journey of progress and inclusive growth.
Jamal Bin Theniyah
Chairman

PROGRESS WITHOUT PAUSE
Emaar sustained its strong growth momentum across the quarters in 2025, recording strong property sales, 
rising profitability and an expanding revenue backlog. We made remarkable advances in elevating customer 
experience, promoting sustainability and developing talent. Marked by upgraded credit ratings, new project 
launches and talent development initiatives, the year’s performance reinforced Emaar’s reputation as a global 
developer creating enduring value and thriving communities. 
Setting the Pace for the Year
In Q1 2025, Emaar delivered robust performance with property sales of approximately 
AED 19.3 billion, underscoring its operational excellence and distinctive brand appeal. 
We focused on customer satisfaction, talent development, cost efficiency and sustainability, 
achieving an MSCI ESG rating upgrade to ‘A’ and launching multiple projects across 
the UAE, Egypt and India.
Balanced Growth with Discipline
In Q2 2025, Emaar reported steady growth across all business segments, driven by strong 
property sales, rising profitability and an expanding revenue backlog. Credit ratings were 
upgraded to BBB+ (S&P) and Baa1 (Moody’s). We continued to prioritise customer delight 
while focusing on building Emirati talent through targeted initiatives, including the 
launch of our first Youth Council.

Exceptional Delivery, Enduring Confidence 
Emaar demonstrated a strong Q3 performance as well, achieving higher revenue and 
profitability across its property, retail, and hospitality segments. We also received an ‘A’ 
rating in our MSCI ESG ratings during this period. The momentum reflected our disciplined 
focus on quality, customer satisfaction and timely execution – qualities that continue to 
draw investors and first-time buyers to Emaar communities. With this trajectory, Emaar 
continues to set the benchmark for integrated, high-quality urban development across the 
region and beyond.
Resilient Demand, Record Returns 
Emaar closed Q4 2025 with strong momentum, converting robust sales and disciplined 
execution into record revenue and profitability for the year. Demand across its master-
planned communities remained resilient, supported by timely project progress and 
operational efficiencies that sustained healthy margins across the portfolio. The quarter 
reinforced Emaar’s financial strength and long-term growth visibility, positioning 
the Group to continue delivering high-quality urban destinations and consistent 
value for shareholders.

=== página 12
SHAPING THE WAY  
THE WORLD WILL LIVE
Strong Growth and  
Strengthened Foundations
For Emaar, 2025 was another year 
of disciplined execution and solid 
commercial performance. In 2025, we 
recorded property sales of AED 80.4 
billion and revenue of AED 49.6 billion, 
with EBITDA of AED 25.6 billion and 
net profit before tax of AED 25.7 billion. 
Our revenue backlog from property sales 
reached approximately AED 155 billion, 
providing strong visibility and reinforcing 
the resilience of our long-term model.
Our UAE development business delivered 
AED 71.1 billion in property sales and 
expanded its revenue backlog from 
property sales to AED 134.3 billion. 
Performance was supported by successful 
launches, including the recently unveiled 
Grand Polo Club & Resort master plan, 
and continued momentum across 
The Oasis, Dubai Hills Estate, The Valley 
and Dubai Creek Harbour. Construction 
across key developments remained 
ahead of schedule, reflecting disciplined 
governance, operational efficiency and 
effective supply-chain planning.
In today’s environment, speed and quality 
are no longer competing priorities—they 
are expected together. Recent advances 
in prefabrication, robotics and highly 
automated construction processes 
reinforce what is possible when delivery 
is ‘industrialised’ with precision and 
repeatability. We are actively evaluating 
how to bring more of these methods 
into our development ecosystem, where 
appropriate, to compress timelines, raise 
quality consistency, and strengthen 
cost discipline—while maintaining 
the design excellence that defines 
Emaar communities.
Recurring revenue assets further 
strengthened earnings. Emaar Malls 
reported revenues of approximately 
AED 5.8 billion, average occupancy above 
98% and record visitor numbers. We are 
also advancing the strategic expansion 
of our recurring revenue portfolio, 
including the Dubai Mall expansion and 
Dubai Square at Dubai Creek Harbour, 
underscoring our confidence in Dubai’s 
long-term position as a global retail and 
lifestyle destination.
Our hospitality, leisure, and entertainment 
portfolio generated AED 4.8 billion in 
revenue, supported by higher occupancy, 
improved ADRs, and sustained demand for 
branded lifestyle experiences. We expanded 
the portfolio with three additional hotels 
offering over 750 keys during 2025, 
continuing to enhance the experiential 
dimension of our communities.
Dear Shareholders,
Emaar is closely linked to the growth story 
of the UAE—a nation that continues to lead 
the region in economic diversification, 
technological advancement, and human-
centric development. As the UAE 
progresses its ‘We the UAE 2031’ vision, 
strengthens global tourism, accelerates 
digital adoption, and reinforces its 
position as one of the world’s safest and 
most resilient economies, it is also setting 
benchmarks that global cities increasingly 
seek to follow. This context shapes a real 
estate market defined by confidence, 
maturity, and long-term opportunity.
Dubai’s residential market continues 
to deepen across mid-tier, premium, 
and ultra-luxury segments. Sustained 
population growth, global investor interest, 
and rising expectations for lifestyle-led 
living are driving demand for integrated 
communities rather than standalone 
homes. This reflects a broader global shift 
toward placemaking and experience-driven 
development—an evolution that Dubai is 
helping to lead.
Emaar has played a defining role in 
this transformation. Through iconic 
destinations and carefully master-planned 
communities, supported by a diversified 
platform across development, retail, 
hospitality, and entertainment, we focus on 
creating enduring models for how people 
will live, work, and connect in the future. 
This positions us strongly for the UAE’s 
next phase of progress—one increasingly 
shaped by artificial intelligence, 
innovation, and a relentless focus on 
execution and efficiency.
We are shaping the next generation of 
communities, including The Heights 
Country Club & Wellness and Emaar 
Estate, aligned with evolving expectations 
around mobility, well-being, sustainability, 
and integrated living.

-   MANAGING DIRECTOR’S 
STATEMENT
=== página 13
International operations delivered 
AED 9.3 billion in property sales, led 
by strong performance in Egypt and 
India. In Egypt, we launched Marassi 
Red Sea—a landmark 2,426-feddan 
master plan with an investment of EGP 
900 billion—combining residential 
communities, luxury hospitality, retail, 
a marina, and integrated leisure offerings. 
This was complemented by continued 
progress at Mivida Gardens, where 
strong demand underscores the appeal 
of our established urban communities 
in New Cairo. In India, demand for our 
premium communities remains strong. 
Together, these markets allow us to extend 
the Emaar model internationally while 
maintaining consistent standards of 
design, execution, and governance.
Beyond near-term performance, our 
international presence and growing land 
bank provide a foundation for future 
profitability and capital deployment. 
We continue to deepen our footprint 
across select international markets 
where long-term urbanisation trends, 
demographic growth, and rising 
aspirations support sustained demand. 
This measured expansion, anchored in 
large-scale master plans and disciplined 
capital allocation, enhances earnings 
visibility and positions international 
operations as an increasingly meaningful 
contributor to long-term value creation.
Throughout all of this, our first 
responsibility remains clear: to 
generate sustainable, growing returns 
for shareholders through disciplined 
investment decisions, strong governance, 
and uncompromising execution.
Building Future-ready 
Communities
Our strategy is built around long-term 
value creation rather than short-term 
cycles. We remain focused on high-quality 
master plans, phased launches and 
a balanced mix of development and 
recurring revenue assets, ensuring growth 
that is both scalable and sustainable.
We are shaping the next generation of 
communities, including The Heights 
Country Club & Wellness and Emaar 
Estate, aligned with evolving expectations 
around mobility, wellbeing, sustainability, 
and integrated living. These developments 
reflect our conviction that the future of 
real estate lies in complete ecosystems, 
not individual assets. We continue to 
expand our UAE land bank to secure a 
strong pipeline for the decade ahead, 
while pursuing international opportunities 
with clear structural demand.
Digital transformation remains central 
to future readiness—and we are moving 
decisively from ‘digital enablement’ toward 
an AI-led operating model. Artificial 
intelligence is reshaping how work gets 
done across industries, and we see it as a 
major opportunity to raise productivity, 
reduce cycle times, and improve decision 
quality at scale. We are embedding 
automation, advanced analytics, and AI 
across design, construction, property 
management, retail, hospitality, and 
support functions to enhance efficiency, 
deliver predictive insights, and elevate 
customer experience—while strengthening 
cybersecurity and data governance as 
foundations of trust and resilience.
Just as importantly, the human side 
of this transformation matters. As AI 
changes job content and eliminates certain 
tasks, success will increasingly belong to 
teams who know how to use these tools 
intelligently. We are prioritising capability-
building and upskilling so our people can 
operate, govern, and continuously improve 
an increasingly automated enterprise.
Embedding Responsibility into 
Performance
Our results reinforce the importance 
of aligning operational excellence with 
a clear ESG strategy. In 2025, Emaar 
achieved an improved MSCI ESG 
rating of ‘A’—our fourth upgrade in five 
years—reflecting broad-based progress 
across environmental, social, and 
governance dimensions.
We continued to strengthen supplier 
oversight and enhance site safety 
through targeted training, strengthened 
compliance, and closer coordination 
across contractors and HSE teams. 
Sustainability initiatives advanced 
through wider adoption of energy- and 
water-efficient systems, improved 
construction waste management, and 
continued progress toward LEED 
certification across select communities.
We also improved asset performance 
through enhanced operational 
efficiency and stronger energy and 
resource management, expanded clean 
energy integration, and continued to 
evaluate more sustainable materials 
and construction methods. Customer 
experience remained a priority, 
supported by digital enhancements 
and a strengthened Responsible Brand 
Representation Policy. Our commitment 
to community engagement continued 
through initiatives that improve 
connection, inclusion and long-term 
social value.
Our scale, execution capability and 
integrated portfolio give us the confidence 
to continue shaping exceptional 
communities and world-class lifestyle 
destinations, while delivering sustained 
value to our stakeholders.

=== página 14
In 2025, Emaar further strengthened its 
role as a key driver of Dubai’s growth, 
awarding contract works valued at 
AED 27 billion and contributing around 
AED 6 billion to the Road and Transport 
Authority and other government entities. 
Our malls welcomed an impressive 204 
million visitors across all assets, including 
110 million to Dubai Mall alone, while our 
hospitality assets hosted over 9 million 
guests. At the heart of our community 
impact is the Emaar Foundation, which 
continues to serve as our key platform 
for meaningful social contribution and 
long-term community support. Through 
the Emaar Foundation, we proudly 
dedicated AED 12.6 million in 2025 and 
over AED 70 million in the last three 
years to impactful social initiatives, 
reinforcing our commitment to people-
focused development and inclusive 
progress. More broadly, we advanced 
our CSR agenda by delivering initiatives 
valued at approximately AED 17 million 
in 2025, inclusive of contributions made 
through the Emaar Foundation, reflecting 
our commitment to creating sustainable 
value for the communities we serve and 
supporting Dubai’s long-term development 
vision. Our 2026 New Year celebrations 
set a new global benchmark, transforming 
Downtown Dubai into a week-long 
spectacle from December 31 to January 
7, offering approximately 2.8 million 
visitors an immersive experience that 
showcased the city’s exceptional energy 
and creativity. Dubai Opera, the city’s 
cultural heartbeat, also welcomed over 
250 thousand visitors in 2025, further 
enriching Dubai’s vibrant cultural and 
entertainment landscape.
The UAE real estate sector continues 
to demonstrate depth, resilience and 
structural strength. Population growth, 
sustained global investor confidence, a 
maturing luxury segment and Dubai’s 
continued appeal as a centre for business, 
innovation and lifestyle underpin 
long-term demand. As the nation 
advances smart governance, digital real 
estate systems, and sustainable urban 
development, Dubai increasingly serves 
as a reference point for the cities of the 
future—a context in which Emaar is 
well positioned to lead.
Our scale, execution capability and 
integrated portfolio give us the confidence 
to continue shaping exceptional 
communities and world-class lifestyle 
destinations, while delivering sustained 
value to our stakeholders. In parallel, we 
are selectively exploring opportunities 
to invest in international yielding 
assets, with plans to deploy capital in a 
disciplined manner, subject to projects 
meeting our return thresholds and 
offering attractive, double-digit returns 
on invested capital, thereby enhancing 
long-term portfolio resilience and 
income diversification.
I thank our people, customers, and 
partners for their continued trust and 
support. Your confidence enables us to 
think long-term, act responsibly, and 
continue building places that enrich lives 
and communities for generations to come.
Mohamed Ali Alabbar
Managing Director

Showcasing Dubai on the World Stage
Emaar’s concentrated roll-out of landmark projects and retail investments—including 
the ongoing Dubai Mall expansion, the announcement of AI-enabled & electric 
vehicle-friendly Dubai Square Mall in Dubai Creek Harbour and multiple 
high-profile launches across Dubai Creek Harbour, Dubai Hills and The Oasis—has 
amplified Dubai’s appeal as a global leisure, retail and residential destination. Strong 
property sales and record visitor numbers to Emaar destinations have reinforced 
Dubai’s magnetism for international buyers and tourists, helping position the city as 
a premier global hub. 
Building Momentum on Sustainability
Sustainability continued to shape how we strengthen performance, manage future 
risks, and deepen trust with our stakeholders. From progress on climate action 
aligned with national priorities to initiatives that reinforced community connection, 
our approach reflects a focus on resilience, responsibility, and long-term growth 
across the business. 
Paving the Path to a More Prosperous Future 
Emaar translated higher property sales and disciplined execution into a materially 
larger revenue backlog and improved profitability in 2025, strengthening its revenue 
and profit visibility and cash flow runway. Upgraded credit ratings (S&P and 
Moody’s) and rising EBITDA/revenue metrics underscore improved creditworthiness 
and investor confidence that support long-term capital planning.

Source: Central Bank of the U.A.E., Quarterly Economic Review, September 2025.
Dubai’s Residential Market 
Maintains Upward Momentum
Dubai’s real estate sector delivered its 
strongest performance on record in 2025, 
surpassing 270,000 transactions valued at 
AED 917 billion, reflecting a 20% year-on-
year increase. Supported by transparent 
regulations, disciplined market practices 
and a long-term investment outlook, 
the sector has evolved from a phase of 
rapid expansion to one of sustainable 
market leadership.
These outcomes also indicate steady 
progress towards the Dubai Real Estate 
Sector Strategy 2033, which targets a 
70% rise in transaction volumes to reach 
AED 1 trillion. This trajectory aligns with 
the Dubai Economic Agenda D33, aimed 
at doubling the emirate’s economy and 
reinforcing Dubai’s standing among the 
world’s leading economic centres.1
During 2025, total sales transactions 
reached 215,458, marking an 18.9% 
year-on-year increase compared with 2024. 
Residential properties continued to anchor 
market activity, contributing 93.9% of 
overall transactions, broadly consistent with 
the previous year. Growth was primarily 
driven by apartments (+21.7%) and villas 
(+20.5%), largely supported by strong 
off-plan demand, with under-construction 
units significantly outperforming 
completed stock. Commercial real estate 
also gained momentum, particularly in 
the office segment, where transactions 
surged by 53.6% amid sustained 
demand and a continued shortage of 
high-quality inventory.2
On a full-year basis, 2025 witnessed an 
unprecedented acceleration in development 
activity, with 648 project launches 
introducing more than 167,000 units

GROWTH FUELLED BY OPPORTUNITY
Economic Overview of the UAE
In the first quarter of 2025, the UAE economy grew by 3.9% year-on-year, supported 
by the strong performance of the non-hydrocarbon sector, which is primarily driven by 
manufacturing, financial services, construction and real estate activities. The outlook 
remains positive, with real GDP growth forecast at 4.9% for 2025 and 5.3% in 2026 
alongside sustained momentum in the non-hydrocarbon sector. The forecast for non-
hydrocarbon GDP growth for 2026 further reflects the potential indirect benefits of 
robust hydrocarbon activity, including increased investment, government spending and 
overall economic confidence.

to the market, valued at approximately 
AED 463 billion, equating to a new launch 
every 13.5 hours. A total of 258 developers 
initiated projects during the year, a 40% 
year-on-year increase from 2024, signalling 
a continued broadening of supply-side 
participation. Apartments remained central 
to new supply, accounting for 88.8% of units, 
while villas and townhouses represented a 
rising share of total launch value, reflecting 
sustained demand for higher-value, 
lower-density products.
In comparison, 2024 recorded 481 launches 
and just over 145,000 units valued at 
AED 360.1 billion. The scale and pace of 
activity in 2025 highlight the market’s 
transition into a structurally higher supply 
cycle, particularly within the apartment-led 
off-plan segment.
Limited Retail Supply with 
Steady Pipeline1
New retail supply in Dubai remained 
limited during 2025, a trend expected 
to continue into 2026, with no major 
retail schemes scheduled for completion. 
The next meaningful expansion of 
inventory is anticipated from 2027, when 
major projects such as are expected to 
collectively add over 240,000 sq m of 
gross leasable area (GLA). In Abu Dhabi, 
retail additions in 2025 were similarly 
constrained, largely comprising 
community and neighbourhood 
developments totalling around 
60,000 sq m of GLA. Looking ahead to 
2026, there is only one major scheme in 
the pipeline, contributing approximately 
51,000 sq m of retail GLA, alongside 
smaller community-led developments. 
Beyond this, longer-term projects such as 
are set to shape future market dynamics 
and enhance the region’s retail offering.
Sustained Growth  
in the Tourism Sector2
Between January and December 
2025, Dubai welcomed 19.59 million 
international overnight visitors, 
representing a 5% year-on-year increase 
and reaffirming the city’s strong global 
tourism appeal. Visitor demand remained 
geographically diversified, led by 
Western Europe (21%), the GCC (15%), 
South Asia (15%), and CIS and Eastern 
Europe (15%), followed by MENA (11%), 
North-East and South-East Asia (9%), 
the Americas (7%), Africa (5%), and 
Australasia (2%). Supporting this growth, 
Dubai’s hospitality sector operated 827 
establishments with 154,264 rooms, while 
average occupancy improved to 80.7% in 
2025, up from 78.2% in 2024.

Revenue 
Emaar’s achieved its highest-ever revenue 
for the year reaching AED 49.6 billion, 
reflecting an increase of 40% compared 
to 2024. This performance was driven 
by steady construction progress across 
multiple developments, robust retail and 
hospitality operations, and continued 
growth in population and tourism. 
Property sales remained resilient, and we 
achieved our highest-ever property sales 
of AED 80.4 billion in 2025, an increase of 
16% over 2024 sales of AED 69.5 billion, 
reflecting Emaar’s strong brand and solid 
demand across our projects and master 
communities. Meanwhile, our revenue 
backlog reached approximately 155 billion 
as of 31 December 2025, a year-on-year 
growth of 39%, providing strong visibility 
on future revenue at healthy margins.
Gross Profit
We achieved a gross profit of 
AED 27.2 billion in 2025, reflecting a 
year-on-year increase of 34%, with healthy 
gross profit margins across the portfolio. 
This performance was supported by 
operational discipline, effective cost 
management and a continued focus 
on enhancing construction efficiency. 
Despite higher activity levels and 
inflationary pressures, margins remained 
resilient, underpinned by strong pricing, 
a favourable product mix and ongoing 
cost-efficiency initiatives.

EBITDA
EBITDA for the period stood at 
AED 25.6 billion, representing a 33% 
year-on-year increase. This performance 
was supported by operational efficiencies, 
and healthy margins across all business 
lines and prudent expense management. 
The growth in EBITDA continues to 
reflect the strength of our diversified 
operating model.
Net Profit
Emaar recorded its highest-ever net 
profit of AED 22.3 billion in 2025, 
representing a year-on-year increase 
of 28%. This result reflects strong 
revenue conversion, healthy margins and 
disciplined financial management across 
the Group. Profitability was supported by 
the substantial revenue backlog, sustained 
demand across operating segments 
and continued cost optimisation, 
despite the introduction of corporate 
taxation in the UAE.
Debt
Total debt stood at AED 9.8 billion as at 
31 December 2025, while cash and cash 
equivalents, including short-term deposits 
amounted to AED 71.5 billion, reinforcing 
a strong liquidity position. The Group 
continues to maintain conservative 
leverage levels, resulting in a net cash1 
position of AED 61.7 billion. This robust 
liquidity, together with available undrawn 
facilities, supports our balance sheet 
strength and its capacity to fund ongoing 
development, recurring asset expansion, 
and shareholder returns.

UAE  
DEVELOPMENT
KEY STRENGTHS: PROVEN EXECUTION CAPABILITIES  |   
MASTER DEVELOPMENTS  |  PRODUCT INNOVATION  |   
TARGETED MARKETING  |  STRATEGICALLY POSITIONED LAND BANK
1 Including JVs.
Emaar’s development approach focuses on expanding and enhancing 
master-planned communities across the UAE to meet the rising demand 
for high-quality, connected living environments. Supported by a substantial 
and well-located master-planned land bank, including approximately 
344 million sq ft, the Group is well positioned to scale developments in 
line with evolving market demand. By leveraging strategic land holdings 
and strengthening existing developments, Emaar continues to deliver 
well-planned master communities that combine residential, retail, 
hospitality, and recreational elements. Optimised designs, enhanced 
amenities, and diversified product offerings ensure relevance across 
customer segments, supporting sustainable growth while contributing 
to the maturity and appeal of the UAE real estate landscape.

-  UAE DEVELOPMENT
=== página 26
Business Performance
Emaar continues to shape the UAE’s urban landscape through large-scale, integrated 
master developments that feature remarkable lifestyle destinations and iconic assets. 
In 2024, we strengthened our land bank with the acquisition of approximately 
140 million sq ft, including the Grand Polo Club and Heights Country Club master plans, 
with an estimated development value of AED 96 billion. Its luxury portfolio was 
further enhanced with the launch of Emaar Estate. Strong demand across new and 
existing communities drove operational momentum, supported by rapid sell-outs, 
efficient project delivery and improved digital engagement.
Financial Performance
In 2025, Emaar recorded its highest-ever UAE property sales of approximately 
AED 71.1 billion, reflecting strong market demand and execution strength. 
As of 31 December 2025, the revenue backlog from property sales in the UAE stood 
at around AED 134.3 billion, providing visibility on future revenues and reflecting 
sustained demand for Emaar's integrated communities and premium lifestyle offerings. 
Disciplined construction and delivery supported UAE development, consolidating 
revenues (including Dubai Creek Harbour) of AED 36.4 billion during the period. 
Together, these results highlight the robustness of Emaar’s financial position and 
reinforce its leadership in the UAE real estate market.
Outlook
Emaar remains focused on consolidating its strengths and preparing for 
the next phase of growth. The continued rollout of major new master plan 
communities, such as The Heights Country Club & Wellness and Emaar 
Estate, together with additional phases across existing developments 
including Dubai Creek Harbour, Dubai Hills Estate, The Oasis and The 
Grand Polo Club & Resort, provides strong visibility on future development 
activity. Strategic land acquisitions in prime locations, supported by the 
long-term vision of Dubai’s D33 Agenda and the Dubai 2040 Urban Master 
Plan, position the development business for sustained growth, profitability 
and continued demand from both local and international buyers.

-  MALLS AND COMMUNITY RETAIL
=== página 29
Business Performance
Emaar's mall and community retail business continues to deliver consistent growth, 
establishing itself as the preferred gateway to Dubai’s consumers for leading retailers 
and global brands. Its malls offer a distinctive urban lifestyle experience, benefiting 
from mature catchment areas created through Emaar’s integrated master community 
developments. Renowned for their superior positioning and strong footfall, Emaar’s 
malls remain the top choice for retailers and a preferred destination for both residents 
and tourists. The opening of Dubai Hills Mall in February 2022, with approximately 
2 million sq ft of GLA, remains a signature example of Emaar’s ability to deliver 
transformative retail and lifestyle landmarks that resonate with communities. Emaar is 
advancing the development of Dubai Square in Dubai Creek Harbour, a landmark retail 
and entertainment destination featuring innovative technologies, unique concepts and 
elevated dining, shopping and leisure experiences.
Financial Performance1
Emaar Malls demonstrated strong results, recording a revenue of approximately 
AED 5.8 billion and EBITDA of AED 4.9 billion, supported by healthy operating margins 
and average occupancy exceeding 98%. Growth in tenant sales across multiple categories, 
alongside rising footfall driven by the success of the luxury, lifestyle and retail segments, 
underscores the sustained attractiveness of Emaar’s portfolio. These results reflect the 
strength, quality and resilience of Emaar’s retail assets and highlight their ability to 
maintain relevance and profitability in a competitive market environment.
Outlook
The retail portfolio continues to pursue a multi-format expansion strategy, 
leveraging Emaar’s strong residential communities, high-quality asset 
mix and deep sector expertise. Future growth will be supported by 
enhancements to existing assets, including the Dubai Mall expansion 
and the development of new formats such as Dubai Square at Dubai 
Creek Harbour. Emaar remains focused on strengthening rental income, 
improving operating efficiency and elevating the customer experience 
through innovation and continual asset upgrades.

Emaar offers a diverse portfolio of world-class hospitality and leisure

=== página 32
Business Performance
Emaar’s hospitality, leisure, commercial leasing, and entertainment businesses continued 
to demonstrate robust growth in 2025, as underscored by the addition of three new 
hotels with over 750 keys during the year. The portfolio also grew with the addition of 
one VEO gym, reflecting our commitment to lifestyle diversification. We maintain a 
strong pipeline of 23 hotels, over three-quarters of which operate under management 
contracts, ensuring operational excellence as well as brand consistency. Renowned labels, 
including Address Hotels & Resorts, Palace Hotels & Resorts, Vida Hotels & Resorts and 
Rove Hotels, continue to deliver strong ADRs and occupancy levels, compared to Dubai’s 
market averages. 
Financial Performance
The hospitality, leisure and entertainment portfolio generated revenue of AED 4.8 billion, 
achieving a growth of 12% compared to 2024, underpinned by robust performance in 
accommodation, dining and leisure experiences, alongside stronger occupancy levels and 
an uplift in ADR.
Outlook 
 
Steady growth in tourism and strong domestic spending continue to 
support a positive outlook for Emaar’s hospitality, leisure and entertainment 
businesses. With Dubai’s growing appeal as a global lifestyle, tourism and 
financial hub, occupancy levels across Emaar’s hospitality and leisure 
portfolio are expected to remain robust. Supported by a healthy pipeline 
of new hotels and an expanding recurring-revenue portfolio, the segment 
is well-positioned for sustained growth, strong revenue visibility and 
continued improvement in profitability.

Business Performance
International development operations delivered a stable performance in 2025  with 
property sales primarily driven by strong demand across Egypt and India, supported 
by key landmark project launches. Furthermore, we have a strong pipeline of units 
under construction to support future launches. The business continued to benefit from 
disciplined execution, selective new launches and a substantial international land bank 
that supports long-term growth. Emaar’s established community-development model, 
combined with prudent cost management and steady construction progress, ensured 
resilience across markets despite varying macroeconomic conditions.
Outlook
Emaar will continue to apply its proven and resilient business model across 
international markets, focusing on the expansion of development, retail and 
hospitality operations in high-potential regions. A substantial international 
land bank underpins long-term growth prospects, particularly in key 
markets such as Egypt and India, where Emaar is enhancing its community, 
retail and hospitality pipelines. The strategy remains centred on maximising 
land bank potential, strengthening market presence and creating vibrant 
lifestyle destinations aligned with local demand trends.

=== página 37
Sustainability and innovation continue 
to be critical to our evolution as Dubai’s 
leading real estate developer, and are 
embedded in every aspect of our business, 
from the way we design and build to how 
we operate, engage and create value for 
all stakeholders. Guided by the UAE’s 
strategic approach to sustainability 
and aligned with global sustainability 
frameworks, our approach combines 
responsible governance, environmental 
performance and social impact to amplify 
long-term, tangible results. This progress 
was further evidenced by an improvement 
in our MSCI ESG Rating to ‘A’ in 2025, 
representing the Group’s fourth upgrade in 
five years and underscoring the continued 
strengthening of our ESG performance.
In 2025, we continued to translate 
this vision into action, advancing our 
environmental goals, strengthening our 
governance systems and deepening our 
commitment to people and communities.
We developed a Climate Risk Framework 
to systematically identify and assess 
physical and transition risks, embedding 
climate considerations into enterprise 
risk management. Building on this 
foundation, we defined a comprehensive 
decarbonisation strategy and roadmap, 
which is aligned with the UAE Net Zero 
by 2050 strategic initiative and global 
climate change goals.
The year also marked the launch of 
Phase 2 of the Energy Optimisation and 
Retrofit Programme, targeting high-
energy-intensity assets across business 
units to enhance operational efficiency 
and reduce emissions. Furthering our 
commitment to renewable energy, Emaar 
Hospitality Group signed a landmark 
Solar lease agreement to integrate clean 
energy capacity across four flagship 
recreational facilities.
OUR PROGRESS TOWARDS A 
RESILIENT FUTURE
Our mission is to shape the future of real estate by creating spaces that 
enrich lives and stand as enduring symbols of progress. Our developments  
are more than structures; they are thriving communities designed to 
connect people, foster well-being and inspire the next generation of 
sustainable urban living.

-  OVERVIEW
=== página 38
We continued to evaluate the potential 
of using more sustainable materials and 
green building design methods and how 
we can integrate low-impact construction 
practices and resource-efficient systems 
into our developments. Several projects 
advanced toward or achieved Green 
Building certifications, reinforcing 
our commitment to sustainable urban 
development. A key milestone during the 
year was Dubai Mall’s achievement of 
LEED Platinum certification under the 
LEED v4.1 Operations and Maintenance 
rating system, which is one of the 
highest international benchmarks for 
sustainable building operations. This 
recognition reflects sustained excellence 
in energy and water efficiency, indoor 
environmental quality, and responsible 
asset management. With Burj Khalifa 
having achieved this certification in the 
previous year, two of our flagship assets 
now hold this distinction.
Strengthening governance and 
accountability remained a central pillar 
of our Group ESG strategy. In 2025, we 
updated our Corporate Governance Policy, 
strengthening oversight of regulatory 
obligations, internal controls and 
decision-making processes across the 
Group. We also revised and updated key 
corporate policies, including the Anti-Fraud 
and Whistleblowing Policies, ensuring 
alignment with international best practices.
We enhanced our commitment 
to responsible marketing and 
communications by replacing our previous 
policy with a revised Responsible Brand 
Representation Policy, thereby ensuring 
our brand is represented with integrity, 
inclusivity, and environmental and social 
responsibility across all markets.
In addition, we reinforced our commitment 
to protecting and promoting human rights 
by integrating these considerations into 
governance and risk management systems. 
During the year, we developed a Human 
Rights Strategy and Roadmap, Human 
Rights Risk Register, and Human Rights 
Policy. These will enable the systematic 
identification and management of potential 
risks across operations and supply chains.
Our social agenda remained focused 
on inclusion, empowerment, and 
engagement. We enhanced the employee 
experience through expanded training 
and career development opportunities, 
all while maintaining a relentless 
focus on health and safety. Across our 
communities, we promoted sustainable 
living through awareness campaigns on 
energy conservation, water efficiency, and 
waste segregation, fostering a culture of 
environmental and social responsibility.
We also continued to set new benchmarks 
in customer service and engagement. 
Through initiatives such as our ESG 
Customer Survey Insights 2025, 
we deepened our understanding of 
customer expectations, which enabled 
us to make targeted improvements that 
enhanced satisfaction, transparency and 
sustainability engagement. With our 
Emaar One app, we served over 98,000 
active users, processed more than AED 
7.2 billion in payments and achieved a 
significant reduction in customer walk-
ins, reflecting our ongoing shift toward 
seamless digital engagement.
In line with the UAE’s Year of Community,

(CSR) initiatives, the Emaar Foundation, 
and community-focused initiatives, 
we deepened our community impact, 
investing in programmes that promote 
education, inclusion and environmental 
stewardship, while delivering meaningful 
support to vulnerable groups and 
communities in need.

Looking Ahead
As we look to build on this momentum, sustainability will remain central 
to our growth strategy, and will drive innovation, operational excellence 
and long-term value creation. In the period ahead, we will focus on the 
phased implementation of our decarbonisation strategy and roadmap, 
supported by strengthened climate risk governance and continued 
operational improvements. In parallel, we will begin rolling out our 
newly established Human Rights Policy and Strategy, starting with UAE 
operations, to further embed responsible business practices across our 
operations and supply chain. By integrating responsible governance, 
environmental action and social progress, we are shaping the urban 
spaces of the future, resilient, inclusive and designed to thrive for 
generations to come.
=== página 39
EMAAR’S ESG APPROACH
We have developed a Group-wide ESG 
strategy and framework built on a robust 
foundation of governance mechanisms to 
identify and manage material ESG risks 
and opportunities. 
Based on a comprehensive assessment 
of our material ESG topics developed in 
consultation with key stakeholders, the 
strategy framework sets clear priorities to 
ensure a focused approach to navigating 
the next stage of our sustainability 
journey. The following sections of 
the report showcase how we have 
embedded sustainable and responsible 
business practices as an integral part 
of our business culture.
ESG Governance and 
Oversight
ESG governance at our Company is overseen 
by the ESG Steering Committee, which 
comprises senior executives, and reports 
directly to the Chairman of the Board of 
Directors (Emaar Properties – Parent Level).
The Committee oversees the effective 
identification, assessment and management 
of material ESG risks and opportunities, and 
their strategic integration within business 
decisions, enabled by cross-functional 
collaboration between business units. ESG 
performance is monitored and assessed 
periodically through Steering Committee 
reviews, ensuring accountability at the 
highest levels.
Each member of the Committee has 
specific ESG-linked Key Performance 
Indicators (KPIs) integrated into their 
performance reviews and remuneration 
structures. These KPIs encompass critical 
areas, such as customer and employee 
satisfaction, leadership succession 
planning, talent management, energy 
efficiency and health & safety, reinforcing 
our commitment to sustainable and 
responsible business practices.  
The Committee is supported by the 
Group ESG Department, which leads 
the implementation of our Group ESG 
strategy, ensuring that sustainability 
principles are embedded across all 
business units and integrated into 
our overall strategy.

In addition, we have established three Working Groups, comprising representatives from 
all business units, to enable strategic actions across priority areas:
Climate Change and Environment 
Working Group
Evaluates and refines our Climate 
Change and Environment Roadmap, 
and identifies emerging climate risks 
and opportunities, ensuring proactive 
adaptation and mitigation strategies.
ESG Reporting and Data Capture 
Working Group
Enhances the accuracy and credibility 
of ESG data through streamlined 
reporting processes and shares 
best practices and lessons across 
departments to improve performance.  
The Working Group aligns with global 
frameworks such as the GRI Standards, 
and works to improve transparency and 
accuracy in reporting.
Human Rights  
Working Group
Identifies human rights risks pertinent 
to our operations and supply chain 
and proposes mechanisms to manage 
such risks and strengthen supplier 
accountability.  
including the Group ESG Policy. 
The Policy outlines our ESG priorities 
across environmental stewardship, 
social value creation and governance 
excellence, defines key roles and 
responsibilities, and details a 
Group-wide framework designed to 
embed sustainability into every aspect 
of our strategy and operations. 
Moving forward, we will continue 
to review and enhance governance 
structures to ensure that ESG is fully 
aligned with the business strategy and 
integrated across operations.

To redefine excellence globally by creating transformative experiences that inspire, connect, and enrich 
communities, while driving innovation and sustainability for a thriving future.
Exceptional places to live

development and community management.
Exceptional places to visit

shifting consumer preferences by embedding sustainability.
Exceptional places to work

improves productivity through strong health and safety practices.
Exceptional places for the environment

by a comprehensive materiality

in consultation with over 100 key 
stakeholders, which identified

manage and report on.

stakeholder engagement and 
materiality assessment guide the 
direction of the ESG strategy, 
enabling us to address critical 
challenges while maximising social 
and environmental impact.

prioritised the 24 ESG material 
topics, nine of which were 
identified as high-priority.

ESG-RELATED RISKS
ESG-related risks are integrated into 
risk management practices at the 
operational level across the Group 
and within each business unit. 
Such risks may relate to significant 
factors, including our reputation, 
cash flows and overall business 
prospects, which can ultimately 
translate into financial risks.
As part of this process, risks are assessed 
and prioritised using an impact and 
likelihood matrix, aligned with risk 
appetite. Mitigation plans are then 
developed and communicated across all 
levels of the organisation for each risk, 
ensuring a comprehensive and holistic 
risk profile with organisation-wide 
visibility. This process is governed by 
an Enterprise Risk Management (ERM) 
framework, ensuring consistent oversight 
and management of ESG risks.
In 2025, we extended the scope of ESG 
risk management to include change risks 
(physical and transition) and human 
rights risks (on the business and on risk 
holders) in more detail. The management 
of these topics is addressed in the 
Climate Action and Human Rights 
subsections, respectively.
ESG risk management is further enhanced by maintaining key ISO certifications, including: 
ISO 14001:  Environmental 
Management System
ISO 55001: Asset  
Management System

sustainability, governance and innovation across  
our operations. 
Sustainability principles are integrated into project design through energy-efficiency 
measures, smart technologies and the pursuit of green building certifications. We address 
climate change through investments in renewable energy and emissions-reduction 
initiatives, and we prioritise social value creation by safeguarding the interests of 
workers and customers and promoting community well-being. Innovation and digital 
transformation are integrated across operations, while robust risk management and 
governance frameworks are designed to strengthen resilience.

Supporting Global Goals
As Dubai’s leading developer of 
integrated communities, we align 
with the Sustainable Development 
Goals (SDGs) and focus on 
areas where the Group can have 
the greatest impact through 
our operations. The following 
examples illustrate how our 
approach to sustainability supports 
progress toward these goals.
76,946 hours of training, while we 
also extend education and awareness 
programmes to residents, tenants, and 
community members.
SDG 5 – Gender Equality
We are committed to empowering women, 
who now represent 26% of the Group’s 
workforce. We also celebrate International 
Women’s Day and Emirati Women’s Day to 
acknowledge their growing role in shaping 
our success.
SDG 6 – Clean Water and Sanitation
We support responsible water use and 
reuse across our developments. In 2025, 
we expanded the use of treated wastewater 
for landscaping and enhanced irrigation 
efficiency through smart control systems 
that reduce consumption by as much as 
5% at equipped sites.
SDG 7 – Affordable and Clean Energy
We are advancing the transition to 
clean energy across our assets and 
communities. During the year, we signed 
a 1.46 MWp Solar Power Purchase 
Agreement for major hospitality 
and leisure facilities, contributing to 
long-term emission reductions.
SDG 8 – Decent Work 
and Economic Growth
As the UAE’s major developer of integrated 
communities and a leader in the hospitality, 
entertainment and retail sectors, we 
provide thousands of direct and indirect 
employment opportunities across our UAE 
and international operations, contributing 
SDG 3 – Good Health and Well-being
We safeguard the health and safety of 
employees, customers, and visitors. In 
2025, our Company stayed true to this 
commitment by conducting a total of 
1,672 health and safety training sessions, 
providing 941.5 hours of instruction to 
12,863 employees across our Hospitality, 
Malls, and Entertainment business units.
SDG 4 – Quality Education
We promote lifelong learning and skills 
development across our workforce 
and wider stakeholder network. In 
2025, our employees completed over 
Thanks to various waste management 
initiatives deployed across business units, 
we achieved a 65.8% landfill diversion 
rate during the year in the UAE on average 
across Malls, Community Centres, 
Hospitality, Burj Khalifa, Dubai Fountain, 
and Communities.
SDG 13 – Climate Action
We recognise our role in addressing 
climate change through strong mitigation 
and adaptation measures. In 2025, we 
introduced a comprehensive Climate Risk 
Framework and decarbonisation strategy 
and roadmap to support the UAE's Net 
Zero ambition by 2050.
SDG 16 – Peace, Justice 
and Strong Institutions
We strengthened our governance 
framework in 2025 with an updated 
Corporate Governance Policy, Anti-Fraud 
Policy and Whistleblowing Policy to 
enhance transparency, accountability, 
compliance, and ethical conduct across 
all operations.
SDG 17 – Partnerships for the Goals
We collaborate with other stakeholders, 
including government entities, NGOs, and 
industry peers, to amplify sustainable 
development and shared value creation. 
Examples include our active participation 
in the Emirates Green Building Council 
(EGBC) and the Indian Green Building 
Council, as well as our participation in the 
Unity for Change initiative, which aims to 
shape unified sustainability standards for 
the UAE retail sector.
significantly to local economies in our 
countries of operation. We directly employ 
8,291 employees across six countries.
SDG 9 – Industry, Innovation 
and Infrastructure
Our communities and projects are 
supported by a network of energy-and 
water-efficient district cooling plants. 
By optimising load management, district 
cooling supports long-term urban 
sustainability and contributes to more 
resilient, low-carbon communities.
SDG 11 – Sustainable Cities 
and Communities
We apply innovation and technology 
to improve efficiency and reduce 
environmental impact across our 
developments and communities. In 2025, 
Dubai Mall achieved LEED Platinum 
certification, while we also advanced 
our Energy Optimisation and Retrofit 
Programme across high-energy assets 
and were recognised as the ‘Sustainable 
Retrofit Project of the Year’ at the MENA 
Green Building Awards 2025, organised 
by the Emirates Green Building Council.
SDG 12 – Responsible 
Consumption and Production
We promote circular economy principles 
across our operations. We encourage 
waste segregation and recycling across our 
communities, while the Emaar Hospitality 
Group collaborates with Reloop, a digital 
platform that collects food waste from 
hotels and restaurants, transforms it into 
compost, and diverts it from landfill.

Our approach to safeguarding the environment focuses on 
addressing the most pressing challenges for business units and 
stakeholders. We are committed to minimising our environmental 
footprint across the full spectrum of business activities, from 
land acquisition to community development and operational 
management. Commitments are enshrined in the Group ESG 
Policy and are further outlined in our Climate Change and 
Environmental Policy, which was developed this year.

environmental risks and opportunities.

Group, which shares best practices 
and learnings across departments to 
improve performance.

management systems appropriate to

a coherent and focused approach to 
environmental management across the 
Group. Emaar Facilities Management

Climate Change Mitigation 
and Adaptation
We recognise climate change as a material 
business risk and a global challenge that 
requires decisive action. Our strategy 
focuses on mitigation by reducing 
Greenhouse Gas (GHG) emissions through 
energy efficiency, low-carbon design 
and renewable energy integration, and 
on adaptation by ensuring buildings are 
designed with high temperatures and water 
scarcity in mind. Through these efforts, 
we aim to contribute to national and global 
climate goals while safeguarding long-term 
value for stakeholders.
The UAE Federal Government is 
targeting a 79% reduction in building-
related emissions by 2035 (versus the 
baseline) as part of the UAE Net Zero by 
2050 strategic initiative. As one of the 
country’s leading real estate developers, 
we are well-positioned to support the 
achievement of this objective.
The Board Risk Committee reviews 
climate-related risks and opportunities 
as part of overall enterprise risk 
management and business planning, as 
and when required, while the Group-level 
ESG Steering Committee, reporting to 
the Chairman of the Board of Directors, 
oversees climate action and energy 
management strategies. In 2025, we 
strengthened climate change governance 
by developing a Group Climate Change 
and Environmental Policy.
Supported by working groups and 
task forces, the ESG Department is 
responsible for driving implementation 
of our climate strategy across all business 
units, overseeing and coordinating policy 
development, setting decarbonisation 
roadmaps and targets, managing data 
collection and stakeholder engagement, 
and ensuring alignment with the UAE 
Net Zero by 2050 strategic initiative, 
national regulatory requirements, and 
global frameworks.
Business units in the UAE will lead 
operational planning based on 
departmental KPIs and targets and 
implement the decarbonisation strategy, 
roadmap and operational initiatives. 
Engineering and facilities management 
(FM) teams will ensure compliance 
with standards and execute site-level 
initiatives. Some business units also 
follow dedicated policies aligned to the 
Group’s overall strategy. For example, 
Emaar Malls’ FM team operates under 
its own Energy and Environment Policy, 
while Emaar Community Management 
adheres to a consolidated policy covering 
Energy, Environmental and Health & 
Safety matters.
Accelerating Our  
Climate Journey
In 2025, we advanced our climate change 
journey, strengthening our approach 
across operations in the UAE, aligned 
with the UAE’s Net Zero ambitions. 
Moving forward, these steps lay the 
foundation for more effective climate 
action and mitigation across the Group’s 
UAE operations.
Phase 1 – Baseline and Planning
The first phase involved a comprehensive 
review of the Group’s existing approach 
to addressing climate change, including 
the GHG emissions inventory and existing 
policies and commitments. Key insights 
were also captured through internal 
stakeholder discussions.
The purpose was to build a compelling 
business case for strengthening climate 
risk management and the development of 
a Group-wide decarbonisation strategy, 
aligned with the UAE’s Net Zero by 2050 
strategic initiative.

GHG Emissions Inventory
Our baseline GHG emissions inventory, 
conducted in 2024 in accordance with 
the GHG Protocol, established a clear 
boundary and a unified methodology for 
carbon accounting across key markets 
and business units.
We engaged closely with an array of 
internal and external stakeholders to 
establish the baseline GHG emissions 
inventory. Furthermore, we developed 
clear procedures and tools to ensure that 
we systematically gather comprehensive, 
high-quality data. As a result, we 
established emissions inventories 
for key markets, including the UAE, 
India, and Egypt.
As part of the above-mentioned Phase 1, 
we conducted a comprehensive review of 
our existing GHG inventory. This review 
resulted in a restatement of previously 
reported emissions, reflecting a revision 
of the reporting boundary following 
enhancements to the emissions calculation 
methodology and data accuracy.
Scope 1 emissions across the UAE, India, 
and Egypt totalled 18,406 tCO2e in 2025, 
compared to 10,899 tCO2e in 2024. 
These emissions are largely attributed to 
increased diesel consumption in power 
generators, as well as higher refrigerant 
recharge volumes in AC and chiller 
systems due to maintenance requirements.
Scope 2 emissions across these three 
markets amounted to 681,993 tCO2e in 
2025 versus 598,375 tCO2e in 2024, driven 
by higher demand for grid electricity and 
purchased district cooling across UAE 
business units. The increase in emissions 
was partially offset by an increase in 
renewable energy consumption.
We are continuously evaluating ways 
to further enhance the scope and 
data quality of our GHG inventory 
in line with the recently established 
decarbonisation strategy and roadmap. 
In 2025, we advanced the integration of 
ESG criteria into development project 
procurement processes, enhancing data 
transparency on material sustainability 
issues and strengthening collaboration 
with contractors to improve visibility of 
contractor data.
18,406 tCO2e 
Direct GHG emissions (Scope 1)
681,993 tCO 2e 
Indirect GHG emissions (Scope 2)
Phase 2 – Climate Risk 
Framework
The second phase involved the 
development of a Climate Risk Framework 
to systematically identify physical and 
transition risks, assess exposure and 
impact, and their strategic integration 
into our Enterprise Risk Management 
(ERM) system. This phase also involved 
enhancing our governance structure to 
monitor and manage climate risks.
Phase 3 – Decarbonisation 
Strategy and Roadmap
The final phase involved defining 
science-aligned, actionable 
decarbonisation pathways, with short- to 
long-term milestones and alignment 
with the UAE Net Zero by 2050 strategic 
initiative as well as global standards 
and frameworks. Phase 3 also involved 
assessing the cost-effectiveness of 
decarbonisation initiatives and the 
investment required.
Our 2024 baseline emissions are 
estimated at approximately 0.61 million 
tonnes of CO2 equivalent (tCO 2e), with 
UAE operations accounting for 90% of the 
total. We aim to reduce emissions by 63% 
by 2035 and 90% by 2050 in the UAE.
The decarbonisation strategy and 
roadmap will be supported by the ongoing 
decarbonisation of regional power 
grids, advances in clean technology, 
and progressive government policies. 
It encompasses a range of measures across 
emissions sources under both direct 
control (Scope 1) and indirect influence 
(Scope 2).
To reduce Scope 1 emissions, we are 
considering several initiatives including 
transitioning to low-global warming 
potential refrigerants, electrifying 
our vehicle fleet, optimising energy 
use, and expanding on-site renewable 
energy adoption. Scope 2 emissions will 
be reduced through enhanced energy 
efficiency programmes, smart building 
retrofits, and procurement of renewable 
power via Solar lease agreement and 
renewable energy certificates.
To ensure economic viability and support 
the sustainable transition towards 
low-carbon operations, decarbonisation 
initiatives are structured across three 
timeframes. The short-term phase focuses 
on quick wins and operational excellence, 
including energy optimisation and fleet 
electrification. The mid-term phase will 
scale up clean energy procurement—
subject to market availability and regional 
policy development—as well as supplier 
engagement and tenant collaboration 
to meet the 2035 reduction targets. 
The long-term phase will leverage 
transformational technologies and 
innovations to achieve Net Zero status 
by 2050.

Promoting Energy Innovation
Our overarching energy strategy focuses 
on optimising operations, improving 
energy efficiency, reducing consumption 
and maximising renewable energy 
deployment across all assets, in line with 
Dubai’s Demand Side Management (DSM) 
Strategy, which targets a 30% reduction 
in energy and water use by 2030 and a 
50% reduction by 2050. The Design & 
Development team is responsible for 
integrating energy innovations at the 
design stage of project development, 
while Facilities Management teams 
ensure these are maintained and 
upgraded post-handover of the assets.
We maintain an ISO 50001 (Energy 
Management) certification for flagship 
assets, including the Dubai Fountain 
and the Burj Khalifa in Dubai. The new 
decarbonisation strategy and roadmap 
will guide and inform further energy 
management initiatives across the Group 
in the years ahead.
We apply a data-driven approach to 
energy management, deploying smart 
technologies and efficient building systems 
to optimise performance and enhance 
operational efficiency across our assets. 
Continuous monitoring and benchmarking 
support targeted improvements and long-
term energy savings.
In 2023, we launched a comprehensive 
Energy Optimisation and Retrofit 
Programme across our high-energy-
intensity assets. As part of the first phase 
of the programme, implementation has 
been completed at the Dubai Marina Mall, 
community retail centres and the Greens 
& Views communities, while it is ongoing 
at the Dubai Marina communities, hotels 
and recreational clubs.
Phase 1 covers facilities consuming 369 
GWh annually (43% of the portfolio) and 
is projected to deliver savings of 58 GWh 
(7%) and avoid over 23,500 tonnes of 
CO2 emissions.
The second phase of the programme was 
launched in 2025, targeting hotels (the 
Address Creek Harbour, the Vida Creek 
Beach, the Vida Creek Harbour and the 
Palace Creek Harbour) and communities 
(the Downtown, the Dubai Creek Harbour 
and the Dubai Hills Estate) as well as the 
Dubai Hills Mall. Implementation for all 
assets listed under Phase 2 is scheduled to 
commence in 2026. In recognition of these 
efforts, Emaar Asset Management (EAM) 
was awarded the ‘Sustainable Retrofit 
Project of the Year’ award at the MENA 
Green Building Awards 2025, organised 
by the Emirates Green Building Council.
As part of the Energy Optimisation and 
Retrofit Programme, we have signed 
Energy Savings Performance Contracts 
(ESPCs). Key measures include high-
efficiency retrofits, smart automation 
and data-driven optimisation, ensuring 
occupant comfort while achieving 
substantial energy and carbon reductions.
In 2025, Emaar Community Management 
completed the retrofit of 45 older 
chiller units across The Greens & Views 
communities. The high-efficiency 
replacements strive to reduce energy 
consumption, enhance reliability and 
support the realisation of long-term 
environmental goals. The next phase of 
the programme focuses on the Dubai 
Marina communities.
Emaar Asset Management (EAM) was 
awarded the ‘Sustainable Retrofit Project 
of the Year’ award at the MENA Green 
Building Awards 2025, organised by the 
Emirates Green Building Council.

Unity for Change: Transforming 
Sustainability Standards in UAE 
Retail
We are a founding partner of the Unity 
for Change initiative, launched during 
COP28 in collaboration with the Chalhoub 
Group, LVMH SE, the Majid Al Futtaim 
Group and Aldar Properties. The initiative 
unites leading developers and retailers to 
establish shared sustainability standards 
for the UAE’s retail sector, driving collective 
progress toward low-carbon, circular and 
resource-efficient operations. Monthly 
meetings are held to discuss progress, 
jointly review actual savings versus 
agreed targets, address challenges and 
opportunities, and share best practices.
The Unity for Change initiative  
focuses on three core pillars:
I.    Energy Efficiency 
  Reducing consumption and 
emissions through audits, retrofits, 
and renewable energy adoption
II.   Eco-Design
  Embedding sustainability 
into store design and fit-out, 
encouraging low-impact materials 
and modular, reusable elements
III.   Waste and Resource 
Management 
  Standardising waste segregation, 
digital tracking, and diversion 
from landfills
As a key retail and real estate partner, 
we contribute to developing and 
implementing frameworks across our 
mall and hospitality assets, including 
expanding smart waste-tracking systems 
and digital dashboards to monitor 
diversion rates; introducing energy-
efficient lighting and HVAC upgrades 
across the Dubai Mall and other retail 
properties; and piloting water-efficiency 
measures and tenant recycling initiatives 
aligned with eco-design standards.
The partnership aims to achieve zero 
waste to landfill by 2030, deploy 
digitised waste management systems by 
2026, and deliver annual reductions in 
energy and water intensity. Through our 
active involvement, we help establish a 
new benchmark for sustainable retail 
operations in the UAE. The initiative 
reinforces our role in advancing the UAE 
Net Zero by 2050 strategic initiative, 
promotes sector collaboration and 
demonstrates how collective action can 
accelerate positive environmental impact 
across the retail industry.
ESG IN ACTION
Advancing Energy Efficiency and  
Emissions Reductions Across Emaar Malls
Emaar Malls has established internal 
year-on-year energy-saving targets 
in support of the UAE’s Net Zero by 
2050 strategic initiative. The business 
is committed to identifying and 
implementing capital expenditure 
(CAPEX) energy-efficiency projects, 
alongside continuous operational 
improvement measures to reduce energy 
consumption and operational emissions 
across its retail portfolio.
During the year, Emaar Malls 
implemented several targeted initiatives 
to enhance energy performance across 
its retail assets. At the Dubai Hills 
Mall, a series of efficiency measures 
were introduced, including cooling 
enhancements to eliminate hot-spot 
areas, integration of systems into a central 
energy monitoring platform, seasonal 
‘free cooling’ using outdoor air during 
winter months, the installation of space-
temperature sensors to optimise HVAC 
performance, and lift grouping to reduce 
energy use and wait times. 
At Dubai Marina Mall, a chiller is being 
replaced with a higher-efficiency unit, 
along with a refrigerant shift, resulting in 
estimated annual energy savings of 2.28 
GWh. At the Dubai Mall, temperature 
setpoints and operating schedules were 
optimised to reduce electricity and 
chilled-water consumption, while a chiller 
replacement at the Gold & Diamond 
Park will deliver significant annual 
energy savings.
Concurrently, Emaar Malls has initiated 
the phased replacement of high global 
warming potential (GWP) refrigerants 
with low-GWP alternatives as part of its 
climate mitigation efforts, aiming to fully 
eliminate high-GWP refrigerants across 
its operations by the end of 2032.
Collectively, these actions aim to improve 
operational efficiency, reduce carbon 
intensity, and enhance customer comfort 
across the mall portfolio.

Embracing Renewable Energy and 
Green Mobility
As part of our decarbonisation strategy, 
we are expanding our renewable 
energy capacity and advancing the 
rollout of electric vehicle (EV) charging 
infrastructure across our communities 
and facilities.
To unlock renewable energy potential, 
we conduct technical audits and feasibility 
studies at select UAE sites to identify 
priority locations for on-site solar 
installations. Projects demonstrating 
strong technical and financial viability 
are implemented in phases.
At the end of 2025, our installed 
on-site solar energy capacity across our 
communities stood at 13 MWp. Two 
additional solar PV plants—at Arabian 
Ranches 3 Community Centre and The 
Valley Retail—with a combined installed 
capacity of 0.894 MWp, are expected to 
begin generating clean power in the first 
half of 2026.
We are also extending renewable energy 
adoption across other business units. 
The Dubai Mall has installed 1.2 MWp of 
solar capacity, which is expected to begin 
generating electricity in early 2026. During 
the year, Emaar Hospitality Group signed a 
landmark Solar lease agreement to supply 
1.46 MWp of solar energy across four 
premier leisure assets—Arabian Ranches 
Golf Club, Dubai Hills Golf Club, Hayya 
Lakes Club, and Montgomerie Golf Club—
reducing grid dependence and serving as 
a model for future distributed renewable 
energy projects.
In parallel, we continue to support the 
UAE’s transition to green mobility by 
expanding EV charging infrastructure. 
In 2025, 12 new chargers were installed 
across communities, bringing the total 
number of charging ports to 199, with 
an additional 98 installed across Emaar 
Malls sites, strengthening accessibility 
to low-carbon transport options.
13 MWp
Installed on-site solar energy 
capacity across communities
Sustainable Yacht Fuel at the 
Dubai Marina Yacht Club
The Dubai Marina Yacht Club (DMYC) 
partnered with a local biofuels supplier to 
offer sustainable yacht fuel directly to its 
members. The initiative positions Emaar 
and DMYC at the forefront of maritime 
sustainability. The locally produced 
biofuel, derived from used cooking oil 
and other renewable feedstocks, can 
reduce carbon emissions compared to 
conventional marine diesel.
Emaar India Wins Award for 
Eco-Friendly Residential Building  
At the 5th Sustainability Summit & 
Awards in June 2025, Emaar India won 
the ‘ESG Impact Organisation’ Award 
for its Amaris project in Gurugram. 
The project was recognised for its 
sustainable design, which promotes water 
and energy efficiency and, as a result, 
lowers emissions.

Responsible Resource Use
Water Management
We operate in water-scarce regions, 
making responsible and efficient water 
management a strategic priority. Through 
continuous monitoring, investment in 
smart technologies, and collaboration with 
municipal authorities, we strive to reduce 
water consumption, enhance efficiency, 
and ensure compliance with all regulatory 
requirements. These efforts strengthen 
operational resilience, protect public health, 
and contribute to the UAE’s broader water 
conservation and circular economy goals.
Our Facilities Management teams in the 
UAE oversee water management for all 
UAE business units, while international 
entities implement bespoke approaches 
aligned with local regulations. Our Water 
Management Strategy addresses risks 
and opportunities related to water use, 
quality, and infrastructure. It includes 
a Legionella Risk Management Plan to 
mitigate health and safety risks, supported 
by regular testing, periodic tank cleaning, 
and automated dosing systems that ensure 
compliance with regulatory standards.
Efficiency and  
Conservation Initiatives
Domestic water consumption across 
our portfolio is sourced from the local 
utility, Dubai Electricity and Water 
Authority (DEWA). We continuously trial 
and implement innovative measures to 
reduce, reuse, and recycle water. Key 
initiatives include:
 + Sub-metering in communities to 
promote domestic conservation 
and accountability
 + Installation of water-efficient fixtures 
such as aerators and flow reducers
 + Installation of leak detection systems 
to identify inefficiencies
 + Awareness campaigns and newsletters 
that educate employees and residents 
about water-saving techniques
Digital dashboards track daily water 
consumption and compare performance 
against baselines to identify anomalies and 
opportunities for improvement. Preventive 
maintenance of pumps, dosing systems, 
and heat exchangers ensures sustained 
water quality and operational efficiency.
Across malls, entertainment venues, 
and community centres, we prioritise 
responsible water use by installing 
efficient water-closet flushing mechanisms 
and faucets with aerators, significantly 
reducing water consumption across 
facilities without compromising hygiene 
standards. During the year, aerators were 
installed in the washrooms at the Dubai 
Mall to reduce water consumption.
Emaar Development ensures that a 
range of water conservation measures 
are implemented across its construction 
sites, including controlled usage, leakage 
detection and prevention, enhanced 
monitoring systems, regular water 
quality testing, greywater recycling, and 
workforce awareness programmes.

Water Reuse and Landscaping
We emphasise recycling and reusing 
water to promote resource efficiency and 
environmental stewardship. For example, 
community swimming pool backwash 
is reused for car park and hardscape 
cleaning, while treated effluent water 
supplied by the Dubai Municipality is used 
extensively for landscaping and irrigation 
across master communities, reducing 
freshwater demand while maintaining 
green spaces.
A centralised irrigation control system 
automatically adjusts watering schedules 
based on real-time weather data, ensuring 
optimal irrigation and minimising waste. 
We have set a target of a 5% reduction 
in landscape water consumption at 
sites equipped with smart irrigation 
technology. During the summer, when 
effluent water supply shortages may occur, 
seasonal mitigation plans are activated, 
including the deployment of treated 
effluent water via external tanker services 
to sustain irrigation.
Water Effluent Discharge 
Management
All water consumed across our 
developments is discharged into the 
Dubai Municipality drainage network 
via designated pumping stations. 
Effluent discharge is regulated by the 
Dubai Municipality and DEWA, which 
prescribe limits for key water quality 
parameters such as biochemical oxygen 
demand (BOD), chemical oxygen demand 
(COD), total suspended solids (TSS), pH, 
oil and grease, nutrients (nitrogen and 
phosphorus), heavy metals, and other 
hazardous substances. Compliance with 
these limits is mandatory for all facilities 
in Dubai and ensures that discharged 
water does not harm ecosystems or 
public health. In areas without municipal 
discharge networks, wastewater is 
directed to licensed treatment plants, 
where it is treated and reused for 
landscape irrigation.
6,481,477 m3
Total water withdrawal (UAE)
