Responsible Water Use in District Cooling
Emaar District Cooling (EDC) manages 
water resources with a strong focus on 
efficiency, quality, sustainability, and 
maximising the use of treated sewage 
effluent (TSE) to reduce reliance on 
potable water.
Cooling tower usage is optimised to 
reduce evaporation and treatment costs, 
while water leakages are promptly 
detected and resolved to minimise waste 
and protect supply continuity. Water 
quality is continuously monitored to 
ensure compliance with environmental 
regulations, and performance is 
benchmarked against the Regulatory 
and Supervisory Bureau’s (RSB) 
targets, with results consistently 
surpassing requirements.
Lessons learned are also factored into 
the design of future plants, reinforcing 
continuous improvement. Through these 
measures, EDC safeguards water resources 
while delivering reliable and sustainable 
district cooling services.
In Egypt, water management is a strategic 
priority due to the country’s arid climate. 
Emaar Misr has set a target to achieve 
100% self-sufficiency in water supply 
through irrigation water treatment plants 
(ITP), reverse osmosis (RO) desalination 
plants, and sewage treatment plants 
(STP), while maintaining water quality 
within required standards and reducing 
water losses.
To support this approach, key water-saving 
initiatives include operating on-site 
desalination plants to enhance water 
self-sufficiency and reusing treated 
wastewater for irrigation of green spaces 
and landscaped areas. Across these 
developments, flow meter monitoring, 
preventive maintenance, and regular water 
quality testing ensure compliance with 
regulatory standards, minimise losses, 
and optimise efficiency, thereby ensuring 
sustainable water use across all operations.
Rainwater Harvesting and Water 
Reuse in India
Emaar India has implemented rainwater 
harvesting systems across all projects 
to recharge groundwater aquifers and 
mitigate the impact of water withdrawals in 
water-stressed areas. Additionally, tertiary 
sewage treatment plants (STPs) have 
been installed and commissioned on-site, 
enabling the reuse of treated water for 
landscaping and flushing, reducing reliance 
on freshwater resources. During the year, 
Emaar India initiated the installation of 
ultrafiltration across sites, starting with 
Emaar Business Park, to further improve 
water quality and enable reuse for flushing, 
cooling, and landscaping.  
Advancing Water  
Self-Sufficiency in Egypt

Waste Management
We are committed to responsible waste 
management across our operations. We 
go above and beyond compliance with 
laws and regulations to prioritise waste 
reduction, segregation, recycling and 
responsible disposal.
Emaar Asset Management oversees 
waste management across the Group 
and dedicated teams at Emaar Facilities 
Management and Emaar Community 
Management are responsible for its 
implementation across their respective 
operational areas. 
In 2024, we consolidated our waste 
reporting through a centralised digital 
platform, enhancing data accuracy, 
traceability and transparency across all 
operations. In compliance with the UAE’s 
laws, all waste is managed by licensed 
service providers, while hazardous waste 
is handled by specialist contractors.
We actively collaborate with residents 
and tenants to improve on-site waste 
management practices. To encourage better 
results, mall tenants are incentivised to 
reduce waste and enhance segregation 
through awards and annual recognition for 
achieving the highest segregation rates.
Across master communities, including 
Dubai Hills Estate, Downtown Dubai, 
Arabian Ranches 1 & 2, Dubai Creek 
Harbour, Emaar South, Reem and The 
Greens & Views, green waste is collected, 
and sent to a Dubai-based recycling facility 
for compost production.
We promote sustainable waste 
management through ongoing 
resident engagement and awareness 
programmes, including:
 + Two-bin recycling systems for 
villas and on-site segregation 
across communities
 + Use of biodegradable cleaning products
 + Repair and reuse centres for machinery 
and equipment refurbishment
 + Regular communication to promote 
responsible waste handling 
and recycling
At Emaar Malls, a dedicated team uses 
a smart weighing machine and unique 
tenant ID cards to track each tenant's 
waste generation. On-site segregation 
facilities are available to sort and recycle 
waste, reducing the volume that goes to 
landfills. During the year, a 51% landfill 
diversion rate was achieved through these 
efforts. All tenants receive mandatory 
training, and top performers are 
recognised for excellence in recycling.
22.5%
Of green waste composted since 
programme launch in May 2025 (UAE)

Emaar Development began collecting 
waste generation data across new main 
works projects commencing in 2025, 
with the aim of establishing a baseline 
for construction waste.
In addition, Emaar Development 
integrates sustainability principles into its 
construction activities through systematic 
waste segregation and recycling, thereby 
improving on-site recovery rates. In 2025, 
Emaar Development began inserting 
these requirements into new contracts. 
In addition, several contractors working 
on ongoing projects have voluntarily 
engaged in onsite segregation and 
recycling. Emaar Development will 
seek to replicate these practices across 
future developments, embedding 
recycling and upcycling as standard 
construction protocols.
Emaar Hospitality Group engages in 
initiatives designed to address food 
waste. It plans to expand its collaboration 
with Reloop across all Address Hotels 
in Dubai in 2026. Reloop is a digital 
platform that collects food waste from 
hotels and restaurants, transforms it into 
compost, and diverts it from landfills. 
The initiative uses digital technology to 
track monthly food waste from hotels. 
In addition, Emaar Hospitality Group 
collaborates with local food banks to 
repurpose surplus food for charitable 
purposes and continues to strive toward 
the ‘No to Food Waste’ goal. Together, 
these initiatives bring Emaar Hospitality 
Group a step closer to its aim of achieving 
zero food waste to landfill in the UAE. 
Emaar India is deploying organic waste 
converters (OWCs) across all new projects 
to convert biodegradable waste into 
nutrient-rich compost for landscaping.
In 2025, our UAE operations generated a 
total of 268,660 tonnes of non-hazardous 
waste, compared to 230,419 tonnes 
in 2024. The UAE achieved a waste 
diversion rate of 66% across our Malls, 
Hospitality, Burj Khalifa, Dubai Fountain, 
and Communities business units, 
reflecting the impact of enhanced tenant 
engagement, data-driven tracking, and 
improved on-site segregation.

Sustainable Urban Spaces
Sustainable Material Use, 
Design and Construction
We are renowned for creating integrated, 
mixed-use communities that combine 
residential, commercial, retail, 
hospitality and recreational functions 
within cohesive, sustainable master 
plans. We design our developments to 
promote connectivity, walkability and 
community well-being through integrated 
transport networks, open spaces and 
shared amenities.
Recent flagship developments such as 
Dubai Creek Harbour, Dubai Hills Estate, 
and Emaar Beachfront exemplify this 
vision, blending living, working and 
leisure spaces to foster vibrant, inclusive 
urban districts. By integrating retail and 
hospitality within residential settings, 
prioritising pedestrian-friendly layouts, 
and ensuring access to green and public 
spaces, our developments optimise 
land use while enhancing the quality of 
urban life.
In addition, we continue to evaluate the 
use of sustainable building materials 
and how these can combine with 
innovative design and construction 
methods to reduce the Company’s 
environmental impact.
We advance sustainability within the UAE 
real estate sector through our engagement 
with the Emirates Green Building Council 
(EGBC), while Emaar India is a member 
of the Indian Green Building Council 
(IGBC). As a member of the EGBC, 
we contribute to the development of 
voluntary sustainability standards that 
improve environmental performance 
across the industry. We have participated 
in collaborative workshops with federal 
entities, major developers, and solution 
providers to enhance sustainable criteria 
for materials, energy efficiency, and water 
conservation. These collective efforts 
support the integration of sustainable 
design and operational practices across 
the UAE’s built environment.
The Design & Development team 
implements our Building Design 
Standards and Guidelines, which provide 
an internal framework for incorporating 
sustainable design principles into 
developments and renovations, ensuring 
that projects meet expectations on 
sustainability, functionality, and 
aesthetic appeal.
Designers and architects enforce the 
guidelines at the design stage, and the 
site team manages and implements 
them during construction. The 
guidelines apply to buildings and cover 
landscape specifications, soil and water 
management, and lighting standards.
Key features of the guidelines include: 
 + High-performance, energy-
efficient building envelopes with 
climate-resilient materials 
 + Passive design strategies such as 
shading and ventilation 
 + Energy-efficient HVAC equipment 
and systems 
 + Reduced lighting power densities 
and control strategies 
 + Energy replacement through 
renewable energy 
 + EV charging installations
 + Efficient water management 
We are exploring the potential of several 
innovations as part of our commitment to 
sustainable buildings. For example, the 
Design & Development team is evaluating 
the use of low-carbon construction 
materials and has launched pilot projects 
to ascertain their effectiveness before 
considering their wider rollout in 
future projects.
Launched Pilot Projects
To evaluate the use of low-carbon construction materials
As part of this effort, the team has 
reviewed cement alternatives that achieve 
up to 45% lower embodied carbon than 
traditional mixes. Other areas being 
considered include potential partnerships 
with a modular design contractor and 
with suppliers specialising in recycled 
construction waste.
In addition, we are incorporating 
architectural shading features into the 
design of mid- and high-rise residential 
buildings in Dubai to reduce cooling 
requirements. These features—including 
continuous, non-accessible balconies, fins, 
and shading ledges—create a cooling effect 
that reduces energy consumption and 
emissions while enhancing visual appeal. 
Architectural shading also enhances 
interior lighting quality, thus reducing 
the need for artificial illumination. This 
is just one example of how sustainable 
design choices can simultaneously 
meet energy-efficiency goals, enhance 
occupant well-being and elevate 
architectural design.

Milled Asphalt
Was reused to relay a road at 
‘The Oasis’
GFRP
Was used as a substitute for 
conventional steel mesh in the 
construct of a Community Centre 
in The Oasis
Circularity in Road Construction
We conducted a study into the 
environmental and cost benefits of reusing 
3,500 tonnes of milled asphalt to relay a 
road at one of our residential developments, 
The Oasis. By processing and compacting 
the material into a stable road base, the 
project achieved significant cost savings, 
reduced reliance on virgin aggregates and 
diverted waste from landfills.
The approach also enhanced 
environmental sustainability by lowering 
CO2 emissions and conserving resources, 
while improving efficiency through faster 
deployment, reliable compaction, and 
reduced dust, water and noise impacts.
Lightweight Building 
Reinforcement Reduces Emissions
We piloted the use of glass fibre reinforced 
polymer (GFRP) as a substitute for 
conventional steel mesh in the construction 
of a Community Centre in The Oasis 
development in Dubai.
The initiative replaced 1.66 tonnes of 
steel with lightweight GFRP, reducing the 
carbon footprint almost threefold and 
cutting costs by about 10%. Installation 
was faster and less labour-intensive than 
steel mesh due to its lighter weight.
Results confirmed GFRP’s viability as a 
cost-effective alternative, best suited for 
simple, repetitive elements such as slabs or 
precast walls.
Building a Net-Zero Steel Mosque
We are developing our first net-zero steel 
mosque at the Dubai Creek Harbour. 
The project, spanning more than 1,600 
square metres and accommodating up 
to 745 worshippers, will be built entirely 
using net-zero steel, significantly reducing 
embodied carbon. 
Adopting Low-Impact  
Cleaning Products
Emaar Malls has transitioned to 
eco-friendly cleaning chemicals across 
select assets to minimise environmental 
impact and improve indoor air quality. 
The initiative reduces the release of 
harmful substances and volatile organic 
compounds (VOCs), thereby improving 
air and water quality, and extending the 
lifecycle of building materials and surfaces. 
The dilution ratios of the new products 
were adjusted in accordance with material 
safety data sheets (MSDS), resulting 
in reduced environmental impact and 
improved cost savings.

Green Building Certifications
Our commitment to advancing 
sustainability in the built environment is 
underscored by our success in securing 
sustainability-related certifications for 
multiple projects and by our ongoing 
efforts to expand our portfolio of 
certified assets.
Oversight of sustainable building 
certification in the UAE is led by the 
Design & Development team, while 
international entities manage certification 
independently in accordance with local 
standards and regulations.
All our projects are designed and executed 
in full compliance with the Dubai 
Building Code, ensuring adherence to the 
authority’s safety, quality and regulatory 
standards. Additional sustainability 
and green building requirements are 
applied to align with local authority 
requirements, including:
 + Trakhees: Projects must achieve a 
minimum of 40 points on the Trakhees 
Green Building score-based checklist
 + Dubai Municipality (DM): Projects 
are required to meet the Al Safat Silver 
green building rating level
 + Dubai Development Authority 
(DDA): Compliance with the 
Dubai Municipality Green Building 
Regulations (DMGBR) is mandatory, 
as outlined in the DDA Green 
Building Checklist
Leadership in Energy and Environmental 
Design (LEED) Building and Community 
certifications have been implemented 
across select pilot projects and master 
plans. Further analysis is underway to 
determine the most suitable certification 
frameworks for different project types.
In 2025, Dubai Mall achieved LEED 
Platinum certification under the LEED 
v4.1 Operations and Maintenance: 
Existing Buildings rating system, 
representing a significant milestone for 
the Group. This achievement follows 
the LEED Platinum certification of Burj 
Khalifa in 2024 under the same rating 
system, meaning that two of our flagship 
assets now hold this highest level of 
international green building certification.
Additionally, we are targeting LEED Gold 
certification during the design phase 
of Golf Hillside at Dubai Hills Estate 
and LEED Silver certification (LEED 
Communities v4.1) for the Valley West 
master plan.
International business units are also 
committed to sustainable building 
certifications. In India, our commercial 
buildings ‘Digital Greens Tower A & B’ 
achieved LEED Platinum (v4.1, O&M) in 
2025, while Emaar Pakistan is committing 
to meeting the baseline certification 
criteria for LEED certification in all 
new developments.
Sustainability in the hospitality sector is 
also a priority for us, and 12 hotels have 
achieved the internationally recognised 
Green Key Certification, a leading 
standard for environmental responsibility 
and sustainable practices in the global 
tourism industry.
Furthermore, Emaar Hospitality complies 
with Dubai’s Sustainable Tourism (DST) 
standards. Every hotel in Dubai that 
applies for the Dubai Sustainable Tourism 
Stamp undergoes an evaluation, measured 
against 19 sustainability standards. 
In 2025, four properties earned the 
prestigious DST Stamp.
In addition to environment-focused 
certifications, buildings are designed 
with the well-being of occupants in mind. 
As of the end of 2025, 292 buildings and 
35 podiums are certified under the WELL 
Health-Safety Rating, which promotes 
human health and well-being through 
building design.

Revitalising Urban Spaces
Urban revitalisation is embedded in 
our master planning approach, which 
prioritises high-quality infrastructure 
and public amenities that foster 
inclusivity and liveability. Each project 
seeks to support local economic growth 
by creating employment opportunities, 
attracting investment, and strengthening 
local communities.
While brownfield (previously developed) 
or contaminated-site redevelopment is 
not prevalent within the UAE context 
due to limited legacy industrial zones, 
Emaar Development contributes to 
urban regeneration through large-scale, 
master-planned redevelopment of 
underutilised areas within existing 
city boundaries. Developments 
transform previously low-density or 
infrastructure-deficient zones into 
sustainable, inclusive communities with 
integrated transport, public amenities, 
and economic activity.
Through landmark master developments 
across the UAE and international markets, 
we continue to contribute to the long-term 
regeneration and resilience of cities, 
enhancing the quality of urban life while 
promoting sustainable growth.

Dubai Mall achieved LEED Platinum 
certification under the LEED v4.1 
Operations and Maintenance: Existing 
Buildings rating system, representing one 
of the highest international standards 
for sustainable building operations. 
The certification was awarded based 
on strong performance across key 
environmental and operational criteria, 
including energy efficiency, water 
performance, indoor environmental 
quality, waste management, and 
sustainable site operations.
The Mall achieved an overall score 
of 81 out of 100 points, reflecting the 
effectiveness of ongoing asset management 
practices focused on optimising resource 
use, enhancing occupant well-being, 
and reducing environmental impacts at 
scale. While the certification assessment 
was completed during 2025, formal 
confirmation of LEED Platinum status was 
received in January 2026, following the 
final review process.
With this achievement, Dubai Mall joins 
Burj Khalifa as our second flagship asset 
to attain LEED Platinum certification, 
underscoring our leadership in 
sustainable operations across globally 
recognised landmarks.
Emaar India has committed to ensuring 
that all new buildings in the National 
Capital Region achieve green building 
certification. Certification oversight 
is led by the Government Affairs team 
and supported by a cross-functional 
working group comprising the Design, 
Projects, and Contracts, and Procurement 
departments. Green building requirements 
are included in contracts and compliance 
is monitored through biannual reports 
during construction. Once projects are 
operational, Community and Facilities 
Management teams oversee performance 
to ensure certification standards are met.
In addition, Emaar India applied to the 
Indian Green Building Council (IGBC) 
in 2025 for certification of a 25.9-acre 
group housing development in Gurugram. 
The project has since achieved Platinum 
pre-certification, demonstrating 
commitment to sustainable construction 
and high-performance design.

Biodiversity Conservation
Biodiversity conservation is driven by 
Emaar Development, supported by the 
Design & Development and Landscaping 
teams, and guided by the Building 
Design Standards and Landscape 
Design Intensity Strategy. International 
business units maintain their own 
biodiversity approaches, aligned with local 
environmental regulations and contexts.
At Emaar Development, biodiversity 
is integrated from the earliest stages 
of project planning and design. In 
accordance with UAE environmental 
laws, project design consultants conduct 
environmental impact assessments 
(EIAs) on designated plots and master 
plans to identify biodiversity risks 
and opportunities, evaluate potential 
impacts on habitats and species, and 
inform site design to avoid or mitigate 
negative effects.
EIAs are reviewed by project managers 
and submitted to the relevant authorities 
as part of the permitting, environmental 
clearance, and master planning process. 
Emaar Development consolidates the 
outcomes and monitors compliance 
throughout the project lifecycle to ensure 
full alignment with national regulations.
Contractors and consultants are required 
to report on mitigation and restoration 
measures during project execution. 
This ensures biodiversity protection 
is embedded within the development 
process, consistent with UAE legislation 
and best practices.
At the operational level, the Landscaping 
team supports biodiversity conservation 
through plant selection, habitat 
restoration and maintenance practices. 
Key initiatives include native species 
planting programmes and the use of 
drought- and salinity-tolerant vegetation 
cultivated in local nurseries to reduce 
water use and preserve ecological balance. 
These efforts are complemented by habitat 
restoration projects and the expansion 
of green infrastructure to enhance 
biodiversity within our communities.
We engage with regulators, consultants, 
contractors, tenants and communities 
to ensure compliance, inform site design 
and reflect community expectations for 
green and open spaces. Tenant surveys 
across UAE communities provide valuable 
feedback used to enhance landscaping 
and biodiversity conservation. Progress 
is tracked against mitigation plans, 
restoration commitments and ecological 
health indicators where applicable.
Greening India’s Urban Landscapes
Emaar India is undertaking a large-scale 
biodiversity programme to plant one 
million trees by 2028, strengthening local 
ecosystems and mitigating urban heat. 
To date, over 50% of the target has been 
achieved, with 1,645 trees and 532,000 
shrubs and groundcover species planted 
across project locations in India.
The initiative prioritises native species and 
includes extensive landscaping and public 
greenbelt planting in collaboration with 
the Gurugram Metropolitan Development 
Authority (GMDA). A dedicated 
Horticulture team, reporting to the 
Corporate Development Head, oversees 
implementation in coordination with 
project managers.
Tree-planting events held on occasions 
such as World Environment Day and 
Earth Day engage contractors and 
residents, fostering a shared culture of 
environmental stewardship.

MAXIMISING SOCIAL VALUE

for employees, delivering outstanding customer service and 
supporting local communities. We develop world-class projects 
and destinations that foster sustainable urban development, support 
employment opportunities across the value chain and enhance the 
lives of residents, workers and visitors.

Thriving Workforce
Talent Attraction and Retention
Our employees are the heartbeat of the 
organisation and play a critical role in 
our success story. A diverse and dynamic 
workforce of 8,291 employees across six 
countries is the engine that drives growth 
and innovation.
Talent attraction and retention in 
the UAE are governed by the Human 
Resources (HR) department, which 
defines the overarching strategy 
and frameworks that guide people 
management across all business units 
in the country. International business 
units have dedicated HR departments, 
with strategies and policies tailored to 
local requirements.
We have a structured talent management 
strategy for UAE operations that focuses 
on strengthening employee engagement, 
building capabilities, and enhancing 
workplace flexibility. Key initiatives 
include introducing a new Remote Work 
Policy for UAE employees, a structured 
Career Development Framework, and 
the HiPO programme to identify high-
potential employees and place them on 
an accelerated journey, aligned with 
organisational priorities.
The HR Department works closely with 
business leaders to address evolving 
workforce needs, ensuring alignment 
between HR objectives and business 
strategy. Regular HR leadership reviews 
reinforce this alignment and promote 
data-driven decision-making and 
continuous improvement.
To enhance accessibility and transparency, 
we have introduced initiatives such 
as the Ask HR chatbot and structured 
communication processes, enabling 
employees across all entities to access 
consistent policy guidance, raise 
queries and receive timely support. 
These measures strengthen employee 
trust, promote engagement and ensure 
that every individual has the tools and 
resources to thrive and contribute to 
our long-term success.
We attract talent with competitive 
remuneration packages and career 
development opportunities. Compensation 
is aligned with the market and includes 
a performance-related bonus scheme 
for all full-time employees.
Employees undergo semi-annual 
performance reviews, supported by 
360-degree feedback and ongoing 
performance check-ins tailored to role 
requirements. Performance is assessed 
through a combination of KPIs, goal 
achievement against annual targets and 
competency ratings based on defined 
8,291
Total employees (Group)

Labour Standards
We are committed to maintaining the 
highest standards of honesty, integrity, and 
fairness in the workplace. The Standards 
of Conduct Policy outlines the levels of 
professional behaviour expected of all 
employees of UAE entities, regardless of 
their rank or role within the organisation. 
This includes, but is not limited to, core 
ethical principles, workplace conduct, 
anti-bribery, corruption and fraud, 
confidentiality and data protection, use of 
company assets, compliance, and reporting.
In addition, employees must refrain from 
engaging in any behaviour or practices 
that may allow their private interests to 
conflict with our interests. Employees are 
required to declare potential conflicts and 
must sign a ‘Non-Conflict of Interest Form’ 
upon joining us.
Ethical behaviour in the workplace is 
further governed by dedicated policies 
covering specific topics, including 
a Gifting Policy, Anti-Harassment 
Policy, and Employee Misconduct 
and Disciplinary Policy.
The Standards of Conduct Policy also 
details the standards of fair treatment and 
respect that employees can expect from us, 
including mechanisms for raising concerns 
and reporting any grievances.
We foster an inclusive and welcoming work 
environment and have zero tolerance for 
harassment, bullying, or discrimination 
in any form based on characteristics 
such as gender, age, race, religion, 
nationality, physical ability, or other 
factors. Furthermore, our commitment 
to protecting human rights means there 
is no tolerance for forced labour or child 
labour within operations or across our 
value chain. Our approach to protecting 
human rights is covered in detail in the 
governance section.
As per the Grievance Policy, employees can 
submit complaints, report incidents, and 
share their concerns over any behaviour 
they witness that may contradict the 
Standards of Conduct Policy via designated 
channels. Employees can share in-person 
feedback with an HR representative 
or report grievances through channels 
including the HR platform, e-mail, surveys, 
and internal feedback forums.
Reports can be submitted confidentially 
and anonymously, free from fear of 
retaliation. All such cases will be 
investigated in accordance with clearly 
defined procedures, provided they are 
submitted in good faith. In 2025, 41 
grievances were reported and resolved in 
the UAE.
We ensure that all employees are made 
aware of the Standards of Conduct Policy, 
which is periodically reviewed and updated 
to reflect best practices and any changes 
to operations, and to ensure continuous 
alignment with all applicable legal 
obligations. In addition, employees receive 
training on the Standards of Conduct Policy. 
In 2025, we provided 2,550 hours of training 
covering 1,275 employees in the UAE.
2,550 hours
Of training delivered on the Standards of Conduct Policy (UAE)

Employee Engagement
We recognise that engaged employees are 
the foundation of sustained success. We 
continue to foster a culture of connection, 
well-being, and empowerment through 
structured engagement programmes, 
transparent communication, and 
ongoing feedback mechanisms that 
strengthen trust and collaboration across 
the organisation.
Employees are engaged through multiple 
channels, including surveys, mentorship 
feedback, HR support platforms and 
structured engagement activities. 
Insights from these channels directly 
inform key policy and programme 
enhancements, such as the Remote 
Work Policy and expanded learning 
and development offerings.
In 2025, we strengthened employee 
engagement through a broad series of 
wellness-focused initiatives, including:
 + Employee excursions to cultural and 
entertainment destinations around 
the UAE and activities including 
sports days
 + Cultural celebrations and global 
awareness campaigns, including 
Earth Hour, International Women’s 
Day, World Water Day, World Autism 
Awareness Day, and Nurses Day
 + Free health checks, eye checks, and 
webinars on a range of health-related 
topics, and first aid courses
 + An Iftar gathering attended by 
500 employees, which reinforced 
camaraderie, awareness and unity 
within the team
An internal communications strategy plays 
a pivotal role in driving employee awareness 
and participation. Through newsletters, 
intranet updates and engagement reports, 
employees are kept informed about new 
initiatives, survey results and key decisions 
stemming from their feedback. In addition, 
we have an internal ESG communications 
policy and send twice-yearly ESG 
newsletters to keep all Group employees 
informed of initiatives and progress.
Effectiveness is tracked through 
comprehensive HR dashboards that 
monitor metrics including attrition, 
internal mobility, engagement and 
participation in flagship programmes like 
Mentorship and HiPO. Regular reporting 
cycles and leadership reviews evaluate 
outcomes and guide improvements.
Annual employee satisfaction surveys 
provide valuable insights into employee 
sentiment and help measure engagement 
effectiveness across business units. In 
2025, we maintained a strong employee 
satisfaction survey score of 4.5 out of 5, 
reflecting consistently high engagement 
levels and positive workplace culture. 
Continuous feedback from employees, 
mentors, and line managers ensures that 
initiatives remain relevant, impactful, and 
aligned with our broader workforce and 
succession planning objectives.
4.5 out of 5
Employee satisfaction score across 
the Group

engagement and inclusivity. The certification followed an 
independent employee survey, in which 91% of employees 
affirmed that Emaar India is a great place to work.

Diversity and Inclusion
We build teams with diverse backgrounds 
and skill sets, harnessing their unique 
capabilities to drive innovation and 
progress across the organisation and 
better serve a diverse customer base. 
The workforce combines the benefits of 
youth and experience, ensuring a steady 
pipeline of fresh ideas and perspectives, 
while drawing on seasoned professionals’ 
knowledge and wisdom.
We maintain close partnerships with 
leading universities and recruitment 
networks to attract the best young talent, 
and 45% of new hires are under 30. 
Through a variety of career development 
initiatives, we are investing in future 
leaders to support succession planning.
We also place a strong emphasis on 
expanding career opportunities and 
leadership pathways for women. In 2025, 
females represented 26% of the Group's 
total workforce.  
Our workforce comprises employees from 
a wide range of nationalities, reflecting 
our inclusive culture and the global 
diversity of the communities we serve.

Emiratisation
We are fully committed to supporting 
the UAE’s national agenda by identifying, 
developing and empowering Emirati 
talent across all business units in 
the UAE. The Emiratisation strategy 
focuses on attraction, development and 
retention, ensuring that UAE nationals 
are equipped with the skills, exposure, 
and opportunities needed to take 
on future leadership roles and drive 
long-term business success.
To reinforce this commitment, annual 
Emiratisation targets are embedded 
across all business units, supported by a 
structured succession planning framework 
for high-potential Emirati employees. 
This approach ensures talent continuity, 
nurtures leadership readiness and 
strengthens business resilience. Regular 
reviews and reporting mechanisms, 
ensure alignment with national priorities 
and corporate objectives.
We collaborate with top-tier UAE 
universities to strengthen our pipeline 
of young Emirati professionals. Through 
strategic university partnerships, we 
work with these institutions to identify 
high-potential graduates and align 
recruitment with our long-term vision 
for nationalising the workforce and 
driving innovation.
We also engage actively with the national 
talent market through career fairs 
such as Ru’ya, which serve as strategic 
platforms to attract Emirati graduates 
and experienced professionals who 
align with our culture and values.
Talent development remains central 
to our Emiratisation agenda, with 
targeted programmes designed to 
accelerate growth and capability 
building. These include Graduate and 
Internship Programmes, which offer 
structured on-the-job learning and clear 
pathways to permanent employment for 
young Emiratis entering the workforce. 
Our talent management programmes 
for Emiratis are further explored in 
the next section.
Complementing these initiatives, global 
exposure opportunities are offered to 
young Emiratis through secondments 
to our international business units in 
India, Egypt and Turkey, providing 
valuable cross-cultural and professional 
experience. Several Emiratis were 
also appointed to leadership roles, 
demonstrating the strength of our 
development and succession pipeline.

Talent Management and Development
We continuously enhance our Learning 
& Development (L&D) programmes to 
ensure employees are equipped with the 
skills to perform to the highest standards 
and pursue meaningful career growth. 
The L&D strategy focuses on building a 
capable, agile, and future-ready workforce 
by addressing the risks and opportunities 
associated with talent development and 
aligning closely with our business growth 
and innovation agenda.
L&D is governed by UAE HR, which 
defines the overarching strategy, 
frameworks and reporting standards for 
capability building across all business 
units. International entities tailor their 
learning programmes to local needs 
while aligning with Group principles. 
A dedicated Learning and Development 
Policy covers UAE operations.
The L&D team manages Training 
Needs Analyses (TNA), budgets and 
enterprise-wide programmes. Regular 
TNAs at the Group and business-unit 
levels identify current and emerging skill 
gaps, helping map critical competencies 
across functions and enabling proactive 
workforce planning. Learning budgets 
are allocated employee-wise to balance 
strategic priorities with functional 
upskilling, combining short-term 
operational training, medium-term 
leadership, and capability development 
and long-term future skills building.
Effectiveness is measured through 
learning KPIs, post-training evaluations 
and employee feedback, and the results 
are used to refine programme design 
and delivery. This ensures that our 
learning initiatives remain relevant, 
measurable and responsive to changing 
business needs.
We offer a comprehensive suite of L&D 
programmes, from awareness sessions 
on specialist subjects such as ESG 
to leadership training programmes. 
These are delivered through a blend of 
in-person and online learning platforms, 
catering to employees at all levels.
To coordinate learning and development 
across the Group, the Emaar Academy 
was launched in 2024 as a centralised 
platform to conduct all types of training 
programmes identified as part of TNA. 
More than 300 training sessions were 
conducted through the Emaar Academy in 
a span of 12 months.
In 2025, our UAE employees received a 
total of 55,659 training hours, equivalent 
to an average of 15.4 hours per employee.

Training and Upskilling
Across the Group, we deliver a range of 
training programmes to build technical 
expertise and professional capabilities. 
These include digital learning through 
Coursera, internal mentorship programmes 
focused on project management and 
real estate and specialist external 
training in areas such as sound contract 
administration through the International 
Federation of Consulting Engineers 
(FIDIC) guidelines, Building Information 
Modelling (BIM), Advanced Excel and 
CIPS certification for procurement teams. 
Professional and soft-skills development is 
supported through training in emotional 
intelligence and business etiquette, while 
operational training, including basic food 
safety programmes, is delivered across 
relevant business units.
Online Learning
We also offer a range of online learning 
opportunities that enhance the 
accessibility and flexibility of training, 
while empowering employees to engage 
proactively in their learning and 
development journey.
Leadership Training and 
Succession Planning
We safeguard the long-term health 
and success of our business through 
a structured succession planning 
process. We identify potential leaders 
and successors, and provide them 
with tailored guidance and training to 
prepare them for progression within 
the organisation. 
This initiative, overseen by the L&D 
team in collaboration with managers, 
plays an important role in business 
continuity management by ensuring 
we maintain a steady pipeline of future 
leadership candidates. 
In line with this approach to succession 
planning, our Top Talent programme 
aims to accelerate the growth of high-
potential successors for UAE nationals 
and expatriates by equipping them with 
advanced leadership, business, and 
strategic skills. It strives to build a strong 
pipeline of future leaders who can drive 
our continued innovation, excellence and 
long-term organisational success.
ESG Training and Awareness
The ESG Learning & Development and 
ESG Strategy & Communication task 
forces oversee engagement and awareness 
initiatives to ensure all employees have a 
foundational knowledge of the Group ESG 
strategy. Group-wide initiatives include:
 + A mandatory ESG awareness video for 
all employees including leadership
 + Face-to-face induction for all new 
employees, including a section 
dedicated to ESG awareness
 + ‘ESG Login and Learn’ webinars for all 
employees are conducted four times 
per year. In 2025, 1,277 employees 
attended these sessions    
Furthermore, the EHG training academy 
has integrated twice-monthly ESG 
awareness sessions as part of the induction 
programme undertaken by new joiners. 
Talent Management Programmes for Emiratis
Mentorship Programme 
Our mentorship programme enables UAE 
nationals to build their technical expertise 
in various Emaar-specific competencies. 
The Emaar Mentorship Programme 
3.0 is the latest cycle of our internal 
talent development initiative aimed at 
strengthening leadership capabilities 
and accelerating career growth. Strategic 
partnerships with Emirates, Noon, Gov 
of AI, Salesforce, Google and Autodesk 
enhance learning impact.
Certifications 
We support high-potential UAE 
nationals through professional 
development programmes such as 
Chartered Institute of Procurement & 
Supply (CIPS) and Chartered Financial 
Analyst (CFA), enabling them to 
gain globally recognised specialist 
qualifications. These certifications 
strengthen critical capabilities in 
financial analysis, procurement and 
supply chain management, directly 
contributing to our strategic and 
operational excellence. Through such 
initiatives, we continue to invest in 
building a strong talent pipeline aligned 
with the organisation’s long-term growth 
and leadership vision.
Partnership 
We entered into a partnership with 
INSEAD to create a structured 
Emaar-specific leadership development 
programme featuring action learning 
projects and guidance from experienced 
business coaches.
Hospitality Management 
Trainee Programme 
A fast-track programme for 
developing high-performing Emiratis 
for future corporate roles within 
Emaar Hospitality. The programme 
provides structured rotations, targeted 
development and capability building. 
Participants are currently in Phase 2 of 
the training, working on action learning 
projects that address real hospitality 
challenges. Each trainee has been 
assigned a mentor and is undertaking 
action learning projects addressing 
real-life challenges in hospitality, 
such as solar power installation 
and food waste management.

Health, Safety and Well-being
We are committed to creating a safe and 
healthy workplace for all stakeholders, 
including employees, partners, contractors 
and suppliers, as well as safe and secure 
environments for residents and visitors 
across all managed communities and 
facilities. We foster a culture of health and 
safety across all operations, in line with 
applicable laws and regulations.
A Group Health and Safety Policy, 
approved by the Group CEO, provides the 
foundation for our approach to health, 
safety and well-being. The Policy, which 
applies to all Group operations and covers 
all employees and contractors, sets out 
commitments to:
 + Provide safe and healthy workplaces 
for employees, contractors, partners, 
suppliers, residents and visitors;
 + Comply with legal, regulatory 
and international standards, 
as well as codes of conduct and 
stakeholder requirements;
 + Continuously improve health and 
safety management systems through 
audits, reviews and corrective actions;
 + Communicate transparently, 
reporting progress and performance 
against objectives;
 + Equip and empower employees with 
training, guidance and the ability to 
challenge unsafe practices;
 + Foster a positive safety culture focused 
on identifying and managing risks, 
eliminating hazards and preventing 
accidents; and
 + Ensure robust emergency response 
systems, supported by adequate 
resources and oversight from the 
Corporate Health, Safety and 
Environment (HSE) Department.
These commitments ensure a unified 
approach to embedding health and safety 
considerations across Emaar.
The Board has ultimate oversight of health 
and safety matters at Emaar. The Head 
of Emaar Security Group meets quarterly 
with the Board to discuss health and 
safety performance, address risks, and 
implement effective mitigation plans. 
Our HSE team, reporting to the Head of 
HSE, is responsible for enforcing health 
and safety standards across Emaar.
Members of our HSE team are embedded 
within business units to ensure consistent 
application of standards and provide 
technical support, with the exception of 
Emaar Development, which is driving 
health and safety through our standalone 
HSE Department. Quarterly review 
meetings are held with business unit CEOs 
and Asset General Managers to discuss 
performance, share lessons learned and 
identify areas for improvement. Any issues 
that cannot be resolved at the Business 
Unit level are escalated to the Board by 
the Head of Emaar Security Group. 
In addition, formal joint HSE committees 
operate within several business units, 
meeting regularly to review performance, 
address concerns and recommend

=== página 79
improvements, ensuring workers are 
represented and actively involved in 
maintaining safe workplaces.
Some business units and entities maintain 
dedicated health and safety policies 
(Development, Facilities Management, 
Communities, Malls, Entertainment, 
Burj Khalifa, Dubai Opera, India, Turkey 
and Pakistan) and management systems 
tailored to their operational context and 
regulatory environment, while remaining 
fully aligned with the overarching 
Group Health and Safety Policy and 
governance framework.
Health and safety management systems 
are aligned with ISO 45001 (Occupational 
Health and Safety Management System) 
while Emaar Entertainment Group – part 
of Emaar Malls – is fully certified to this 
standard and covers all employees and 
contractors and 100% of operations and 
facilities of Emaar Entertainment. In 
addition, Burj Khalifa, including At the 
Top and Sky Views, is fully certified.
To enhance oversight of health and 
safety management at Emaar, all 
HSE-related activities are tracked 
and monitored. To further streamline 
and enhance these processes, we have 
introduced Salama (Arabic for ‘safety’), 
a data-driven digital platform that will 
enable real-time tracking of critical 
health and safety factors, process 
implementation, and performance. 
The system provides a framework 
based on eight pillars, including Risk 
Assessment, Compliance, Training, 
Inspections, Incidents & Investigations, 
Emergency & Crisis Management, Event 
Security Management, and Contractor 
Management. Initially piloted by Emaar 
Malls, we are targeting a wider rollout of 
this system across the Group, aligned with 
ISO 45001, ISO 14001 (Environmental 
Management System) and other relevant 
certification standards.
At Emaar Development, the Procurement 
Department manages the selection of 
contractors, consultants and architects 
of record and verifies their eligibility 
through a rigorous pre-qualification 
process. Construction contractors are 
contractually obliged to implement a 
comprehensive Health and Safety Plan 
that aligns with Emaar Development’s 
Project Health, Safety, Environment & 
Security Guidelines, which they must 
sign. The Guidelines align with the 
ISO 45001 certification and all local 
regulatory guidelines.
Contractors are responsible for ensuring 
compliance with the Plan, which applies 
to all persons on-site, including workers, 
sub-contractors, consultants and visitors, 
while a third-party safety management 
consultant independently monitors 
the implementation of the Plan by 
the contractor.
Project-level HSE committees are 
established at each site and managed by 
the respective HSE teams. The committees 
comprise workers, execution teams and 
HSE representatives from contractors and 
consultants. Meetings are held regularly 
to review HSE performance, identify and 
discuss site-specific risks and address 
project-level issues. Worker grievances are 
treated as a priority, with anonymity and 
confidentiality maintained, and matters 
are escalated to project management 
where required.
Hazard Identification and Risk 
Management
Our approach to health and safety 
management is built around the 
systematic identification of hazards 
and assessment of health, safety and 
well-being risks and opportunities 
across all business units.
Each business unit maintains tailored 
hazard identification and risk assessment 
procedures suited to its operational 
context, supported by a standard 
hierarchy of controls to ensure consistent 
and effective risk mitigation. Employees 
are actively engaged in these processes, 
including risk and opportunity 
assessments, incident investigations, 
project planning and policy development, 
ensuring that measures reflect operational 
realities and stakeholder feedback.
Risk assessments are conducted in 
coordination with each business unit. 
These assessments are formal and 
comprehensive, with clearly defined 
control levels. Findings are shared 
with the respective business units, and 
follow-ups are managed to ensure that all 
actions are effectively implemented. Each 
business unit records and tracks these 
within its management systems.

Inspections, Audits and Incident Investigation
We enforce regular health and safety 
inspections and audits across business 
units to ensure compliance with legal 
requirements, standards and business unit-
specific health and safety management 
systems and guidelines. Risk-based audits 
covering 100% of operations are conducted 
annually by the Internal Audit team, while 
external audits are carried out for Emaar 
Entertainment and Burj Khalifa as part of 
the ISO 45001 certification. 
Furthermore, relevant authorities conduct 
inspections on specific elements of the 
management systems, which are considered 
external audits. Business units also conduct 
cross-audits to promote shared learning 
and consistency across operations. All 
internal, external and regulatory audits 
are consolidated and reflected in the total 
number of audits reported. Findings inform 
goal-setting, policy updates and continuous 
improvement across health and safety 
management systems.
Employees are encouraged to report 
hazards promptly, without fear of reprisal, 
reinforcing a culture of openness and 
accountability. Incident investigation 
processes are in place across all business 
units to determine root causes, apply 
corrective and preventive actions, and 
prevent recurrence. 
Lessons learned are systematically shared 
and incorporated into operational controls, 
risk assessments and training materials, 
supporting proactive risk management and 
continual performance enhancement.
At Emaar Development, contractors 
and consultants are required to perform 
monthly inspections and audits and report 
findings to the HSE team. Additionally, 
third-party external audits of the 
contractor are conducted as part of the 
ISO 45001 certification process. These 
are reinforced by the HSE team’s own site 
inspections, in addition to those conducted 
by local health and safety authorities.
Incident reporting protocols are 
implemented across all construction 
projects, aligned with regulatory 
requirements. Procedures are in place for 
reporting, tracking and analysing serious 
incidents, such as fatalities, lost time 
injuries and near misses, and contractors 
must report all major incidents within 
10 minutes, submit a preliminary report 
within 24 hours and a detailed report 
within 72 hours.
Investigations are led by competent 
teams based on severity, with root 
cause analyses and corrective actions 
following the hierarchy of controls. 
In the event of injuries or fatalities, 
additional monitoring and inspection 
plans may be put in place. Lessons learned 
must be shared within seven days and 
subsequently integrated into the health 
and safety management system. The 
contractor is responsible for closing 
corrective actions, while the engineer and 
project management consultant (PMC) 
oversee timely resolution and conduct 
regular audits to ensure compliance and 
continuous improvement.

Performance Monitoring and 
Continuous Improvement
We set internal annual health and 
safety goals and targets, covering 
leading and lagging indicators. Health 
and safety performance is monitored 
through structured reporting cycles, KPI 
dashboards, performance evaluations, 
management reviews and audits. A 
systematic effectiveness review process 
is embedded across all health and safety 
procedures to evaluate results, identify 
gaps and update policies, training 
programmes and preventive measures. 
Insights and lessons learned from these 
reviews feed into feedback loops that drive 
continuous improvement and strengthen 
the Group’s overall safety culture.
Through this integrated approach, 
we ensure that risk management, 
performance monitoring and continuous 
improvement work in tandem to 
maintain a safe, compliant and resilient 
working environment across our 
material operations.
Externally disclosed health and safety 
targets at Emaar Properties are applied 
across the most material health and safety 
risk areas, particularly within construction 
given the sector’s inherent health and 
safety risks. Approximately 86% of the 
Group’s construction activities are based 
in the UAE, the vast majority of which are 
undertaken by Emaar Development.
At Emaar Development, contractor 
performance is assessed through a 
structured rating system that focuses on 
the effectiveness of HSE implementation 
and compliance at project sites. Insights 
gathered from the evaluations are 
used to identify gaps and successes in 
health and safety. Emaar Development 
collaborates with contractors and project 
teams to address these areas, ensuring 
continuous improvement in site-specific 
safety management systems. Emaar 
Development’s approach, programmes and 
risk controls, which form part of its health 
and safety management, are geared towards 
achieving a target of keeping the fatality/
workplace injury incident rate below 0.025, 
which applies to all persons on site.
Emergency Response and  
Crisis Management
We adopt a comprehensive Emergency 
and Crisis Management Model based on 
the principles of prevention, readiness, 
response, recovery, and learning, ensuring 
organisational resilience across all business 
units. Prevention is achieved through 
hazard identification, risk assessment, 
operational controls, and compliance with 
legal and authority requirements to reduce 
the likelihood and impact of emergencies.
Readiness is maintained through structured 
emergency planning, defined command and 
communication arrangements, competency-
based training, and regular testing of 
emergency plans. In 2025, we conducted 
282 fire and emergency drills involving 
13,137 people across our Hospitality, Malls 
and Entertainment business units.
Response arrangements enable timely, 
coordinated, and controlled incident 
management through trained emergency 
teams, clear escalation protocols, and 
effective coordination with external 
authorities.Recovery focuses on the 
safe restoration of operations, welfare 
support, damage assessment, and business 
continuity activation to return activities to 
acceptable levels.
And finally, learning is embedded through 
post-incident and post-drill reviews, 
root-cause analysis, and corrective and 
preventive actions, ensuring continual 
improvement of emergency plans, training, 
and operational controls.
Health and Safety Training and 
Awareness
We ensure that all employees and 
contractors receive comprehensive health 
and safety training, aligned with their 
roles and operational environments.
Core programmes include health 
and safety basics, which cover key 
processes and requirements for staff 
and contractors, and Fire Safety Basics, 
delivered internally by the HSE team, 
focusing on prevention, fire systems and 
emergency equipment.
New joiners typically receive a health and 
safety induction, coordinated with the HR 
Department, to familiarise them with our 
policies, procedures and workplace safety 
standards, based on the risk profile of 
their roles. In compliance with regulatory 
requirements, employees also undertake 
firefighting training delivered by Civil 
Defence-approved providers and first aid 
training conducted by Dubai Ambulance 
Authority-approved trainers.
Business units supplement these core 
programmes with specialised training 
relevant to their operational context. 
For example, at the aquarium in Dubai 
Mall, relevant staff receive diver safety 
instruction from qualified diving 
safety trainers, while ride operators in 
entertainment venues are licensed by 
regulators following certified training on 
operational and safety systems.
In 2025, we conducted a total of 1,672 
health and safety training sessions, 
delivering 941.5 hours of instruction to 
12,863 participants across our Hospitality, 
Malls and Entertainment business units. 
These included internal and external 
training and awareness programmes 
covering employees, contractors and 
other stakeholders.

Worker Welfare and Engagement

participation in health and safety 
management across all business units. They 
are engaged in developing, implementing 
and reviewing health and safety systems 
through regular meetings and feedback 
sessions, while key information is shared via

including rest areas, dining spaces, and 
drinking water stations. Emaar Development

resilience and address physical and mental 
well-being.

ESG IN ACTION
500,000+  
Visitors across Downtown Dubai during 
the New Year’s Eve celebrations
Ensuring Safety at Dubai’s 
New Year’s Eve Celebrations
We successfully welcomed over 500,000 
visitors to Downtown Dubai during our 
New Year’s Eve celebrations, ensuring 
the highest health and safety standards 
through meticulous planning and 
coordination with city authorities.
We implemented a comprehensive health 
and safety strategy encompassing crowd 
management, emergency preparedness and 
real-time response protocols. Operational 
zones and trained on-ground teams 
ensured safe guest movement and rapid 
incident response. To further support crowd 
safety and visitor well-being, temporary 
wayfinding structures and a unified 
QR-based navigation system were deployed 
to reduce congestion, improve clarity of 
movement, and enable safer circulation 
across high-density areas.
We extended Dubai Metro hours, provided 
shuttle buses and planned road closures to 
facilitate a smooth and secure crowd flow. 
Designated safety tents offered first aid, lost-
and-found services and support for guests.
These coordinated measures enabled a 
safe and seamless celebration experience, 
underscoring our capability to manage large-
scale events responsibly while prioritising 
visitor well-being and operational excellence.
Empowering Workers Through 
Safety Campaigns
In 2025, Emaar Development launched a 
joint campaign with the RTA to enhance 
pedestrian safety for site workers, 
particularly at the man-machine interface, 
where interaction with vehicles and heavy 
equipment poses high risks. The initiative 
promotes the use of designated walkways, 
safe crossing practices, reflective Personal 
Protective Equipment (PPE) and strict 
adherence to site speed limits.
Workers receive training on maintaining 
awareness in high-traffic zones and 
communicating effectively with equipment 
operators. Engagement is reinforced 
through toolbox talks, awareness sessions, 
visual reminders and rewards for safe 
behaviour, strengthening a culture of 
vigilance and shared responsibility.
Concurrently, the ‘Beat the Heat’ 
campaign focused on protecting workers 
during summer and provided training 
on heat stress awareness, hydration, rest 
cycles, shaded areas and rehydration 
support. Together, these initiatives 
demonstrate Emaar Development’s 
ongoing commitment to worker safety, 
well-being and proactive risk prevention 
across all project sites.

Customers and Communities
Customer Satisfaction
We intend to deliver superior experiences 
to all customers across our portfolio. 
Customer satisfaction is a key pillar of 
our long-term value creation strategy, 
and is embedded across all business units 
through structured systems that measure, 
manage and continuously improve 
customer service. 
Business units span diverse categories of 
customers, including property owners, 
tenants, hotel guests, shoppers and others. 
Each business unit tailors its approach 
to managing its customer base, in some 
cases guided by dedicated policies and 
procedures. However, all share a unified 
commitment to service excellence, 
transparency and responsiveness, in 
alignment with the Group approach. 
Customer satisfaction management is 
guided by recognised frameworks and 
standards. For example, Emaar Malls 
aligns its service excellence framework 
with the ICXS2019 principles and 
ISO 10002 standards for complaint 
handling, ensuring a consistent and 
transparent process across all customer 
touchpoints. In 2025, Emaar Community 
Management and Emaar Development 
Customer Excellence renewed the 
ICXS2019:02 International Customer 
Experience Standard certification, 
supporting customer excellence for 
residential property owners and tenants.
Listening and Responding
We gather feedback through digital 
platforms such as the Emaar One App and 
the Dubai Mall App, as well as through 
direct engagement at various service 
touchpoints. We employ multiple tools and 
metrics, including customer satisfaction 
(CSAT) scores, Net Promoter Score (NPS), 
Global Review Index (GRI), and Guest 
Satisfaction Surveys (GSS), supported 
by regular audits, analytics dashboards 
and service recovery protocols. These 
systems enable real-time monitoring, 
trend analysis and targeted interventions 
to address issues and strengthen brand 
loyalty. In 2025, we achieved an average 
CSAT score of 94% and swiftly resolved 
85,643 customer service requests raised.
Service quality oversight is integrated 
into corporate governance through 
executive leadership, quality management 
teams and cross-functional reviews. 
Data-driven insights inform policy 
updates, training programmes and 
operational improvements ranging from 
upgraded digital interfaces to enhanced 
guest services. 
94%
CSAT score
For example, Emaar Hospitality uses 
review systems and brand education 
programmes to sustain industry-leading 
audit scores, while Emaar Malls leverages 
mystery shopper audits and customer 
feedback to guide service enhancements, 
such as improved navigation systems 
and accessibility.  
Customer surveys for hotel guests and 
residential communities now include 
environmentally focused questions to 
gain a better understanding of their 
environmental priorities. 
  See ESG Customer Survey 
Insights 2025
Lessons learned from customer insights 
are incorporated into policy revisions, 
service design, digital innovations and 
ESG initiatives. Initiatives such as Emaar 
Development’s Customer Forum, which 
enables residents to directly engage with 
senior leadership, demonstrate how 
feedback drives accountability and fosters 
a culture of continuous improvement. 
Through this integrated, customer-centric 
approach, we ensure that satisfaction is 
both a measure of performance and a key 
driver of brand trust, loyalty and long-
term sustainable growth.

sustainability question to guest feedback

sustainability concerns and priorities. 
Results analysed in the second half of the

Additionally, as part of Emaar’s annual

Emaar One App
The Emaar One App continues to 
serve as the cornerstone of our digital 
transformation, redefining how residents 
manage their property and community 
needs. By unifying over 90% of property 
management services into a single 
platform, the app enhances convenience, 
simplifies processes and delivers a 
superior customer experience.
Customers can seamlessly access over 
40 live features, including property 
payments, service requests, no objection 
certificates (NOCs), ownership update, 
statement of accounts, handover 
appointments, community-related 
processes and more. 
With the UAE Pass login and the recently 
launched Arabic interface, the app 
sets new benchmarks for inclusivity, 
accessibility and ease of use for customers. 
For secondary market property transfer 
NOCs, ~80% of total requests are now 
initiated via the app, with turnaround 
times as fast as two hours, well ahead of 
industry standards.  
Beyond property management, Emaar 
One elevates lifestyle and community 
engagement by integrating home services, 
community amenities and visitor access 
through a single touchpoint.
In 2025, we made the following 
enhancements to the app:
 + Had’reen – Virtual Happiness Centre 
feature on Emaar One was launched to 
facilitate online customer interactions 
with customer service executives for a 
faster resolution. Customers can book 
virtual appointment slots rather than 
walking in. To date, more than 2,000 
appointments have been booked
 + Seamless login via the UAE Pass 
 + Priority booking in new sales launches 
for signature customers
 + Universal search for features 
and services
The impact of the app grew substantially 
in 2025. With over 98,000 unique 
active users and a 4.7-star rating on 
the App Store, Emaar One is the #1 
channel for service requests across all 
communities. The platform processed 
more than AED 7.2 billion in payments 
and contributed to a significant drop 
in customer walk-ins, reflecting the 
continued shift towards customer 
preference for digital solutions. 
By offering reliability at scale, 
transparency in transactions, and 
seamless digital living, the Emaar One 
App remains firmly aligned with the 
Dubai 2030 vision.

Responsible Marketing Practices 
We uphold the highest standards of 
honesty, integrity and transparency in all 
marketing and communications practices 
as part of our commitment to maintaining 
trust with customers and preserving an 
impeccable reputation in the market.
In 2025, we revised and updated 
our Group-level Responsible Brand 
Representation Policy, replacing the 
Responsible Marketing Policy and 
establishing clear principles for how the 
brand is represented across all marketing, 
advertising, sales and customer 
engagement activities.
The Policy is overseen by our Corporate 
Brand Growth Department, with advisory 
support from the ESG Department, 
and implemented by brand growth and 
marketing teams across business units. 
Any deviation must be justified, reported 
to, and formally approved by the Group 
CEO and Executive Director. The Policy is 
reviewed at least once every three years, or 
earlier if required by changes in UAE laws, 
regulations or our internal requirements.
The Policy, which is publicly available on 
our website, ensures communications are 
factual, culturally sensitive, compliant 
with local and international standards 
and that the Group’s promotion of its 
developments and services is ethical. 
It applies to all employees, interns, 
representatives and third-party partners 
involved in brand promotion or sales, 
mandating responsible engagement across 
digital platforms and strict protection of 
customer data and privacy.
Starting in 2026, mandatory training 
and awareness programmes will 
ensure employees understand and 
uphold our standards of responsible 
brand representation, marketing, and 
advertising. The training will be delivered 
through an e-learning video titled 
’Responsible Brand Growth at Emaar,’ 
which outlines ethical communication 
practices, consumer rights, sustainability 
principles, inclusivity and data privacy. 
It reflects the key requirements of the 
Responsible Brand Representation 
Policy and includes practical examples 
to help employees apply these standards 
in their daily work. Completion will be 
electronically recorded and tracked by the 
Human Resources Department.
Aligned with our broader sustainability 
objectives, the Policy also promotes 
environmentally responsible marketing, 
prioritising digital channels over print, 
minimising waste and working with local 
partners to reduce environmental impact.
Emaar’s Quality Promise
We are committed to maintaining the 
highest quality standards across all 
operations. Robust governance systems, 
strict inspection processes and continuous 
improvement measures, ensure that all 
products and services meet regulatory 
requirements and customer expectations.
For Emaar Development, this 
commitment is reflected in the delivery 
of high-quality, durable and sustainable 
real estate projects, from design and 
construction to handover, ensuring every 
property meets Emaar Development’s 
standards of excellence.
This commitment is supported by 
rigorous processes within Emaar Design 
& Development, where projects follow 
consistent internal guidelines that ensure 
functional efficiency, architectural 
coherence and alignment with our 
quality expectations.
Consultants are appointed following 
technical screening of their Quality 
Assurance/Quality Control (QA/QC) 
systems and health and safety practices. 
Their performance is monitored 
throughout the project through 
assessments of design quality, 
timelines, deliverables and regulatory 
compliance, reinforced by detailed project 
documentation that defines best practices 
and supports the effective implementation 
of health and safety protocols.
During construction, strict quality 
controls ensure full compliance with 
specifications and standards. Supervision 
consultants conduct regular inspections 
to verify adherence to guidelines issued 
by Emaar Design & Development, Emaar 
Mechanical, Engineering, Plumbing 
(MEP) and relevant authorities.
Near completion, common areas and 
key building systems undergo thorough 
testing in line with Emaar Facilities 
Management requirements, and Emaar’s 
Project Quality Assurance engineers 
evaluate all internal unit areas to 
confirm that finishes, workmanship and 
performance meet internal standards 
before handover.
Emaar Development applies a structured 
warranty mechanism to uphold build 
quality and customer protection. In 
2025, approximately AED 4.2 million of 
warranty-related works were performed 
by Emaar Development, while all works 
for rectifications during the contractors’ 
Defects Liability Period were executed 
directly by contractors under their 
contractual obligations. No charges were 
levied on homeowners for warrantable 
defects, and the structural warranty is 
governed by the UAE’s statutory decennial 
(ten-year) liability regime.

community-focused initiatives with the

Corporate Social 
Responsibility (CSR)

investing in programmes that address social 
and environmental challenges, and inspire

across key sectors and markets.

align with the Group’s ESG strategy. A dedicated 
team within Human Resources coordinates CSR 
activities across all business units in the UAE,

entities adopt their own approaches to CSR and 
community impact, aligned with their operations 
and community needs.

tracking of outcomes and alignment with our 
ESG objectives, laying the foundation for an 
enhanced, purpose-driven CSR programme that 
delivers lasting social and environmental value.

The Emaar Foundation 
The Emaar Foundation serves as the 
charitable and philanthropic arm of 
Emaar Properties’ UAE operations, 
operating as an independent, non-profit 
entity under the supervision of a dedicated 
Board of Directors. The Foundation 
supports the UAE’s sustainability and 
humanitarian goals by funding and 
implementing initiatives focused on 
education, healthcare, social welfare, 
and community development.
Key activities include providing financial 
assistance to underprivileged groups, 
supporting educational and health-related 
establishments, and collaborating 
with government bodies and NGOs to 
maximise social impact. For example, 
the Foundation supports the Al Noor 
Rehabilitation and Welfare Association 
for People of Determination by providing 
financial assistance to students facing 
economic hardships. This sponsorship 
helps the association maintain its essential 
educational and rehabilitation services 
without disruption. The Foundation also 
advances sustainability-focused projects 
aligned with our broader ESG principles.
Through its structured approach and 
partnerships, the Foundation continues 
to strengthen our contribution to national 
development and community well-being. 
In 2025, the Foundation distributed 
AED 12.6 million to a wide variety of CSR 
initiatives, or 44% of the Group’s total 
CSR expenditure of AED 28.5 million 
during the year.

Creating Impactful Communities
We create thriving and inclusive communities that enhance the quality of 
life for residents, workers and visitors. We ensure that these communities 
are safe, sustainable and support day-to-day well-being, and long-term 
liveability. Continuous investment in quality communal amenities and 
facilities further supports positive community impact. 
Our community management approach is 
guided by a defined Community Impact 
and Sustainability Strategy, aligned 
with the Group’s broader ESG and risk 
management frameworks. Oversight 
of community impact management lies 
with Emaar Community Management 
under Group Operations, supported by 
functional teams.  
Risks and opportunities are identified 
through regular assessments, facility 
audits, stakeholder feedback and customer 
satisfaction surveys, with outcomes 
integrated into enterprise risk reporting. 
Issues are addressed through preventive 
maintenance, corrective action plans and 
community improvement initiatives.
Community operations are managed 
through ISO-aligned systems that ensure 
transparency and accountability, including 
the Integrated Community Management 
System (ICMS), Mollak for Real Estate 
Regulatory Agency (RERA) compliance, 
an Incident Management System for 
safety oversight, and a Community 
Sustainability Dashboard to monitor 
energy, water and waste management.
Stakeholders are engaged through 
Owners’ Committee meetings, resident 
surveys and collaboration with 
government entities such as RERA 
and Dubai Municipality, with feedback 
driving continuous improvement. Emaar 
Community Management measures 
performance through defined KPIs, 
internal audits and quarterly reviews, 
with lessons learned integrated into new 
policies and procedures.
Through this integrated approach, 
we ensure our communities promote 
residents' quality of life and sustainability.

Fostering Community Engagement and Well-being
In alignment with the UAE’s Year of Community, in 2025, we organised 
many vibrant community events and initiatives to celebrate the spirit of 
togetherness and foster connections among residents of all ages. 
From cultural celebrations like Hag 
Al Laila, Holi, Diwali, National Day, 
Halloween, to a ‘Winter Wonderland’ 
attraction, each event brought something 
unique and memorable to the community.
During Ramadan, we organised an 
‘Iftar for our Unsung Heroes’ to recognise 
community service providers. Families 
also participated in the ‘Best Eid 
Recipe Contest’, celebrating culinary 
creativity across generations. 
We also focused on inclusivity and 
wellness with a Seniors’ Recreational 
Event, the ECM Swimming Championship 
to promote health and fitness, and 
competitions such as the Father’s Day 
Poem and Eid Recipe Contest. 
Residents also came together to support 
meaningful causes, including blood 
donation drives and the ’Donate Your 
Own Device (DYOD)’ initiative, for 
which we received a Certificate of Social 
& Environmental Impact from the 
organisers. In 2025, our contribution 
to the DYOD initiative supported the 
donation or recycling of 741 electronic 
devices, reducing CO2 emissions totalling 
835.7 kg and supporting access to digital 
education for underserved students. The 
Company also distributed Eid Al Adha 
Sustainability kits to residents, which 
included a plantable pencil, pen and book. 
The Picture-Perfect Garden Competition 
returned this year, bringing together 
passionate gardeners from across our 
communities. From edible gardens 
to creative designs and sustainable 
landscaping practices, the competition 
highlighted the many ways participants 
contributed to greener, healthier and more 
beautiful community environments.
Community events were complemented 
by Emaar Community Management’s 
(ECM) ongoing engagement efforts, 
which focus on regular communication,

Promoting Accessibility and Inclusivity 
We are committed to creating inclusive environments that promote social 
participation and equal opportunity for all. In 2025, we approved a new 
Customer Experience for People of Determination Policy, a landmark 
initiative that provides people of determination with a variety of benefits 
and ensures seamless delivery of services. 
We also place strong emphasis on 
accessibility and inclusivity across our 
communities and attractions, ensuring 
that everyone can enjoy safe, engaging 
and enriching experiences. In Dubai, eight 
Emaar Entertainment attractions, including 
Reel Cinemas, Sky Views Observatory, At 
the Top, Burj Khalifa, Dubai Aquarium & 
Underwater Zoo, Play DXB, Adventure Park, 
KidZania and Dubai Ice Rink, have achieved 
Autism Certification from the International 
Board of Credentialing and Continuing 
Education Standards (IBCCES).
During the year, Dubai Mall launched the 
Zabeel Parking accessibility enhancement 
initiative to ensure compliance with the 
Dubai Building Code (DBC), and provide 
equal access and improved convenience 
for people with disabilities. The project 
promotes safe, barrier-free movement 
and fosters inclusive, user-friendly 
infrastructure that serves all members of 
the community.
We also partner with schools and 
charitable organisations to expand 
community access to our destinations, 
providing free entry to people of 
determination, low-income families, 
and orphanages.

Dubai Opera:  
The Cultural Heart 
of the Community
In 2025, Dubai Opera reaffirmed its 
strategic role as the cultural heart of the 
UAE and a key contributor to Dubai’s 
Creative Economy Strategy. The venue 
presented over 150 performances and 
50 international productions, welcoming 
more than 1,000 artists from across 
the world and offering audiences an 
extraordinary spectrum of artistic 
experiences, from grand opera and ballet 
to musicals, symphonic concerts and 
Arabic music.
Through its strategic partnerships with 
Dubai Economy and Tourism, Dubai 
Culture, and major global brands, 
Dubai Opera has expanded its reach 
beyond the stage, nurturing cultural 
participation, creative education, and 
community engagement. The institution’s 
commitment to accessibility and artistic 
excellence ensures that culture continues 
to serve as a bridge between generations 
and communities.
By championing innovation, diversity 
and dialogue through the arts, Dubai 
Opera continues to inspire, connect, and 
empower people, reinforcing culture as a 
vital force for social cohesion, creativity 
and sustainable growth across the UAE.

Leading with Integrity
Board Oversight and 
Accountability
Governance oversight is exercised through 
the Board of Directors and its committees, 
which provide strategic direction and 
monitor risk management, compliance 
and sustainability performance. 
All policies, procedures and codes of 
conduct are regularly reviewed and 
updated to reflect evolving business 
priorities and stakeholder expectations. 
In 2025, the Board approved an updated 
Group Corporate Governance Policy (CGP), 
encompassing a comprehensive set of 
supporting policies and terms of reference 
that define the detailed procedures for 
implementing the governance framework. 
Board members undergo regular training 
on the commitments outlined in applicable 
laws and regulations, the requirements 
of DFM and SCA as well as the CGP. 
Specialised training has been provided 
in 2025 to all Board members by a 
well-recognised governance institution.
We ensure full compliance with applicable 
governance regulations by providing 
our Board members with structured 
induction and ongoing training. The Board 
is regularly assessed and evaluated, with 
training needs identified and relevant 
subject matter delivered, supporting 
the continuous enhancement of Board 
effectiveness in line with leading 
corporate governance practices.
This section provides a high-level 
overview of governance at Emaar. For 
a more comprehensive understanding, 
readers may refer to the Corporate 
Governance section of the Report.
